PREPARED EXPEDITION-TO-HAVEN FLOW SOURCE CONFERENCE — NOT SENT.

# Expedition-to-Haven flow source audit

prepared-not-sent; bounded source/interface conference

The existing gameplay loop is substantial and remains preserved. This audit supplies26 concrete interface questions,21 existing loop sections,126 actual Player Rulebook expedition paragraphs and260 selected Core economy paragraphs. The original Player Rulebook copies match byte-for-byte, and the expedition text matches the current UTF16 extract exactly. Older line-number/encoding references are retained as history rather than used as present evidence.

Already reviewed Core,combat,healing,body,equipment,crafting andfood rules govern their actual scopes. Their settled conflicts with the old loop must be linked,not reopened. Unresolved Supply,Pressure,Integrity,reputation,transport,settlement andmode-transition details require explicit decisions.

| Case | Interface | Decision needed |
|---|---|---|
| FL-01 | Authority and already settled conflicts | The older Loop includes end-turn death saves,automatic Wound triggers and control/action shorthand. Bind each to the existing reviewed Core,combat,healing and lifecycle authority rather than reopening or duplicating it. Identify genuinely unresolved domain decisions separately. Preserve the old chapter and give the final player flow one explicit governing route. |
| FL-02 | Twelve-person organization and actual team assignments | Keep the12-person expedition cap and distinguish actual encounter participants. Decide whether optimal team-size bands are recommendations or activity prerequisites,who can act in a small expedition,NPC seats,office roles and assistants. A person cannot simultaneously lead,assist,rest and craft using the same hours. Remote team results require actual communication and travel. |
| FL-03 | One campaign clock across all modes | Define a canonical Watch start/end and actual elapsed time for encounters,one-hour rests,eight-hour activities,delays and parallel teams. An encounter consumes part of a Watch rather than generating duplicate elapsed hours. State partial/interrupted activity outcomes and scheduled Rest Debt processing using existing completed-rest guards. Preserve Opening/Horn numbers as source scheduling only; do not review their cosmological cause. |
| FL-04 | Supply demand and actual nutrition | Complete the food/water/fuel/camp-consumable interface for one daily Supply Unit. Existing servings grant only food credit and waterskins hold actual water. Define finite required quantities,partial shortages,daily settlement anchors,donation,containers and storage without charging or feeding a character twice. Preserve Small/Medium1 and Large2 source demand; special anatomy/diet and transformations need explicit source-specific treatment. |
| FL-05 | Provisioning and prices | Bind available ration,water,oil,torch,tinderbox,container and service records to real procurement,not free stock. Core clean-water day prices and meal prices have no printed water volume/meal nutrition conversion. Decide those finite mappings and complete Supply kit price/mass from actual sources or labeled NEW values. Include loss/spoilage and finite magical food/water effects without universal supply creation. |
| FL-06 | Forage and finite local opportunity | Resolve per-team/per-route/per-Watch Sustenance caps,actual gathered food/water versus abstract Supply,critical half-rounded-up output and repeated/parallel teams. Establish what a depleted site means and who certifies refresh. Keep actual catch/harvest donors and ingredients distinct from untyped Supply. A map or route reentry cannot mint repeated output; do not invent species/ingredient availability. |
| FL-07 | Camp establishment and security | Give each camp tier finite required materials,time,location,accumulated successes and storage accounting,including the temporary20,fortified60 andforward150Load capacities. Define whether benefits add or replace,real supply-line capacity,compromise and recovery. Camp labels do not automatically satisfy every safe/secure rest predicate or grant workshops,food,staff,land rights or evacuation. |
| FL-08 | Pressure changes and event identity | Reconcile duplicated pressure clauses in abort,overcapacity,attention androute sections: state actual application frequency and stacking rather than applying the same event twice. Define source IDs for exposed cargo,ongoing pursuit and camp exposure,base-risk floor,cap6 and maximumtwo reductions. A newly exposed lot and unchanged continued carriage must remain distinct. |
| FL-09 | Complication severity | Resolve the source line Roll exceeds Pressure by exactly0 because of a modifier or reroll: equality is ambiguous against natural2–6 Standard andnatural1 Severe. Supply a total finite outcome table for modified/rerolled d6,Pressure0 and6. Decide one complication per due Watch versus immediate crises,and prevent a Severe complication -> Extraction Test -> Severe complication unbounded cycle. |
| FL-10 | Route Progress and mapping | Define when mapping improvements change current-Watch Progress,unknown-route Recon success versus Map Route accumulation,zero-Risk mapping and forward/backward routes. State exact legal count of reconnaissance benefits and how capacity penalties apply. Multiple officers or teams cannot each reapply the same automatic base Progress. |
| FL-11 | Forced march and interrupted travel | Preserve Severe route check,+2Pressure andextraSupply per active character on successful forced march. Decide critical outcomes,ordinary travel/encounter interruption,supply affordability and missing/slow members. Reconcile Strain5 half travel and actual Load inability to travel; a route check cannot silently override a physical carrying prohibition. |
| FL-12 | Extraction declaration and cargo state | Define once-per-actual-extraction initial Pressure from highest Extraction Class and how changing cargo or briefly reentering a site affects it. Keep secured versus hidden/attention/volatile/cursed/living requirements explicit,including actual restraint/consent/care for living people. Cargo classification never grants ownership or changes physical mass. |
| FL-13 | Extraction checkpoints | Resolve tests at crossed Progress thresholds,midpoint/exit/route-transition overlaps,short routes and Severe-complication tests at the same location. Preserve no routine repetition at the same Progress. A Critical Success +1Progress may cross another threshold; decide a finite scheduling rule and record processed checkpoints so reordering or split travel cannot replay them. |
| FL-14 | Extraction failure and eight-hour delay | Preserve the players choosing time-plus-complication versus stated cargo risk after the Director declares stakes. Define whether the eight-hour loss is an actual extraWatch and what work/rest/supply/pressure occurs meanwhile. Set bounded cargo rescue/vehicle/casualty responses and ownership consequences rather than automatic destruction or duplicated time. |
| FL-15 | Retreat resolution | Keep threeEscape-before-threePursuit,lead check,assistance,at mostone ordinaryEscape andpossibletwo onCritical Success. Define opposed/DC procedure,what winning the round means,simultaneous thresholds,eligible actions and one meaningful pursuit threat. Do not combine a full unopposed enemy nova with the source chase abstraction or reset spent encounter resources at the mode change. |
| FL-16 | Encounter participants and threat budget | Separate roster12 from actual combat group. Define party level for mixed-level participants,creatureCR fractions,party sizes outside3–6,reinforcements andcompanion contributions. Preserve the source budgets as provisional and its existing balance limits. Explain objective/hazard/surprise adjustments once and exact bossTenacity scope using current condition/control authority; authored procedure is not empirical success-rate validation. |
| FL-17 | Transport and inventory | Identify missing actual mount,vehicle,crew,capacity,speed,terrain,load andcasualty-transport profiles needed for a first descent. Existing cartstructuralHP does not imply transport capacity. Keep known pounds,Load,Extraction Class,containers and source-specific weight relief separate,with no duplicated container contents or melded portable cargo. |
| FL-18 | Integrity and structuralHP | Resolve the older0–3Integrity model against reviewed mundane/magical structuralHP and sourced repair costs/time. Decide which assets legitimately retain Integrity and exact conversion/interaction,without converting all stored damage to fullHP or repairing twice. Preserve equipment grade,crafted quality,spent resources,main-body identity and unavailable materials. A Repair Watch is not automatically eight independent repair checks. |
| FL-19 | Haven arrival and evidence | Give the TierI fast settlement and full ten-step process executable entry/exit conditions,with actual inspections,quarantine,claims,appraisal and uncertain information. State work time and skill/proficiency eligibility and whether spell identification reveals market price/provenance. Public knowledge,legal permission,mechanical functionality and ownership stay distinct. |
| FL-20 | Correct valuation domain | Use the reviewed equipment/recipe valuation rules for their actual items and Core artifact formula for real artifacts. Resolve the older Loop multipliers only in their surviving scope; no duplicated Gate/grade/quality/provenance/demand factors. A crafted Masterwork outcome is not automatic artifact marketMasterwork. State actual buyer availability and quote expiry; no guaranteed buyback/arbitrage. |
| FL-21 | Currency rounding and transaction ledger | Resolve Loop nearestgp versus reviewed purchaseceilcp/salefloorcp,with finite examples below1gp and negative artifact netvalue. Keep tax,transport,stabilization,liability andfees in one transaction ledger so Core subtraction is not followed by paying the same cost again. Negative appraisal is not by itself an authorized forced debt or a refund. |
| FL-22 | Claims,taxes,sponsors and insurance | Reconcile claims priority,tax base/category,sponsor percentages andgross/net/trueprofit thresholds using exact source order. Define insuredvalue,premium,deductible,partial losses,provenance,caps andone payout per actual covered loss. Fees already paid must not reappear. The source exclusions andwritten-contract exceptions remain; human/player consent to actual contracts is a table decision. |
| FL-23 | Reputation scales and save preservation | Loop uses-5..+5,Reputation guide/runtime use-3..+3,andruntime qualitative bands map<=-2Hated,+2Honored. Decide canonical numeric/qualitative mapping andbackground standing without discarding original scores,unknown faction keys or prior history. Separate faction-specificstanding,Expeditionrenown andinstitutional licenses; no automatic magic-cost modifier. Cosmological resonance-track definitions remain deferred. |
| FL-24 | Haven actions,services and actual clocks | Define one-day HavenTurn versus activeworkhours andrealrest/care schedules,including research,training,crafting,repair,licenses,information anddependence treatment. Preserve longer individual recipe times instead of the generic Brew a Batch one-day shorthand. Determine facility/personnel/material availability andfailure/time costs; a Haven label alone grants no all-purpose workshop or cure. |
| FL-25 | Contraband,coin assay and person status | Compare Core coin-shaving detection/actualvalue with Loop assayDCs andfees; retain both originals anddecide scope. Bind criminal market stages,Heat,bribes andcomplications to actual operations. Compare Core captive half-to-fourLoad with reviewed casualty transport andLoop3/6/12Load; define voluntary passengers separately. Preserve person agency,legalstatus,wages,rescue/evidence records without creating race-based prices or deciding deferred soul-linked currency origins. |
| FL-26 | Advancement and next-session handoff | Use reviewed milestone-or-XP mode andactual class-level progression. Define materially contributing offscreen members,rounding,level-gap changes,scene cap andprevent duplicate rewards for the sameachievement. Finish with a worked objective -> Watch -> encounter -> cargo -> extraction -> Haven -> nextdescent record using the final decisions. Seasonal/cosmology review andnumerical/human validation remain separate. |

This is a bounded source conference prepared while Fishing is under review. It is unsent and grants no new mechanical approval. All source files remain unchanged. The seasonal chapter and cosmology review remain deferred until after the authorized GitHub push and remote verification. A later exact review must validate the authored full flow and worked examples.

EXACT SOURCES AND CURRENT GOVERNING INTERFACES

domain)."}]},"loopSections":[{"file":"DescentReviewChanges/ReviewBible/docs/mechanics/GAMEPLAY_LOOP_CANON.md","sha256":"ba5ebcbdb77a27cb8fde88ba13fd05216d6808b10f7b58b64e6cba54eed62066","heading":"The complete loop","exactText":"## The complete loop\n\nA Decennium Descent campaign repeats the following playable sequence:\n\n1. **Choose an objective.** Select a destination, contract, rescue, hunt, survey, recovery, or Gate attempt.\n2. **Finance the descent.** Self-fund or accept sponsors, loans, insurance, and obligations.\n3. **Build the Expedition.** Organize up to twelve characters across Combat, Reconnaissance, Research, Resource, and Support teams.\n4. **Load the convoy.** Purchase supplies, tools, transport, preservation equipment, and reserve capacity for recovered cargo.\n5. **Enter the dungeon.** Begin the Opening clock and select a route.\n6. **Resolve Watches.** Assign every character an activity for each eight-hour Watch, make team checks, advance the route, consume supplies, and test Pressure.\n7. **Resolve encounters.** Fight, negotiate, evade, research, harvest, rescue, or retreat.\n8. **Manage attrition.** Treat Wounds, repair equipment, establish camps, replace supplies, and decide whether the objective still justifies the risk.\n9. **Choose cargo.** Appraise, preserve, secure, and prioritize what the Expedition can carry.\n10. **Extract.** Reverse or change the route while cargo, casualties, pursuit, and the Horn increase risk.\n11. **Resolve the Haven.** Submit to inspection, settle claims, appraise and sell cargo, pay shares and debts, recover, craft, research, and change faction standing.\n12. **Advance and prepare.** Award XP, improve the Expedition, record discoveries, and select the next objective.\n13. **Close the Opening.** Resolve the season's economic and political consequences and create a Cycle Record for the next decennial Modes of play","exactText":"## 1. Modes of play\n\n### Encounter Mode\n\nUse rounds and turns when seconds matter. A round is approximately six seconds.\n\nEach creature normally receives on its turn:\n\n- Movement up to Speed.\n- One Action.\n- One Bonus Action, but only when a feature permits it.\n- One free object interaction.\n- One Reaction before the start of its next turn.\n\nNo character can gain more than one additional full Action in the same round. Effects granting additional Actions do not stack; the character chooses one.\n\n### Expedition Mode\n\nUse Expedition Watches when hours matter. One Watch is eight hours. Every twenty-four-hour day contains three Watches: Morning, Afternoon, and Evening.\n\nEvery member must be assigned to one activity during every Watch. A character may Rest instead of joining a team.\n\n### Haven and Downtime Mode\n\nUse days or weeks for recovery, crafting, research, business, training, politics, and preparation. One Haven Turn is normally one day unless the activity states otherwise.\n\n"},{"file":"DescentReviewChanges/ReviewBible/docs/mechanics/GAMEPLAY_LOOP_CANON.md","sha256":"ba5ebcbdb77a27cb8fde88ba13fd05216d6808b10f7b58b64e6cba54eed62066","heading":"2. Core resolution","exactText":"## 2. Core resolution\n\nRoll:\n\n`d20 + ability modifier + proficiency, when trained + circumstance modifiers`\n\n### Difficulty Classes\n\n| Circumstance | DC |\n|---|---:|\n| Routine under pressure | 10 |\n| Tier I standard | 12 |\n| Tier II standard | 13 |\n| Tier III standard | 15 |\n| Tier IV standard | 16 |\n| Tier V standard | 18 |\n| Tier VI documented limit | 19 |\n\nUse +3 DC for a hard version of the task and +6 DC for a severe version. Use -3 DC when preparation or superior tools make the task favorable.\n\n### Degrees of outcome\n\n- **Critical Success:** Total is at least DC + 10.\n- **Success:** Total meets or exceeds the DC.\n- **Failure:** Total is below the DC.\n- **Critical Failure:** Total is at least 10 below the DC.\n\nA natural 20 improves the result by one degree. A natural 1 worsens the result by one degree. It never improves beyond Critical Success or worsens beyond Critical Failure.\n\n### Advantage and disadvantage\n\nRoll two d20s and use the higher result for advantage or the lower result for disadvantage. Multiple sources do not stack. If both apply, they cancel.\n\n### Assistance\n\nA trained assistant who can meaningfully participate grants +1. Up to three assistants may contribute, for a maximum +3. An assistant exposed to the same danger suffers consequences on a Critical Failure.\n\n"},{"file":"DescentReviewChanges/ReviewBible/docs/mechanics/GAMEPLAY_LOOP_CANON.md","sha256":"ba5ebcbdb77a27cb8fde88ba13fd05216d6808b10f7b58b64e6cba54eed62066","heading":"3. Expedition objectives and abort conditions\n\nBefore entry, record:\n\n- Primary objective.\n- Up to two secondary objectives.\n- Desired extraction value.\n- Maximum acceptable casualties.\n- Minimum reserve Supply.\n- Latest voluntary return hour.\n- Conditions that cause immediate withdrawal.\n\nWhen an abort condition occurs, each officer may argue to continue, but the Expedition Leader must record the decision. Opening clock and Watches","exactText":"## 5. Opening clock and Watches\n\nThe Opening lasts 2,400 hours. At hour 1,200, the Horn announces the point of no return. Expeditions still inside must withdraw or accept the risk of being sealed below for ten years.\n\nA Watch advances the Opening clock by eight hours. A normal day contains:\n\n- Two active Watches.\n- One Rest Watch for each character.\n\nA character assigned to work during their scheduled Rest Watch gains **Rest Debt**. An attempted Rest Watch interrupted before completion also creates Rest Debt after the emergency scene ends.\n\nRecord a two-Watch countdown. The character must complete one full Rest Watch before the end of the second following Watch. At the end of that second Watch, an unpaid debt causes 1 Strain and remains owed until a full Rest Watch is completed. A character can owe only one Rest Debt at a time; missing another scheduled Rest while already indebted immediately causes 1 Strain and resets the countdown to two Watches. Completing the owed Rest clears the debt. A character cannot remove Strain until completing a safe Rest Watch.\n\n### Short Rest\n\nA Short Rest takes one hour. A character can benefit from no more than two Short Rests between Long Rests.\n\n- Spend Hit Dice as permitted by class rules.\n- Recover one-quarter of maximum RP, rounded down, minimum 1 for a character with an RP pool.\n- An Oracle regains 1 Revelation Charge, up to maximum.\n- End effects that specify a Short Rest.\n\n### Long Rest\n\nA Long Rest occupies one full Watch and can benefit a character only once per twenty-four hours.\n\n- Restore HP according to the Player Rulebook.\n- Restore RP and Revelation Charges.\n- Recover half expended Hit Dice, rounded up.\n- Remove 1 Strain in a secure camp or Haven.\n- Wounds require treatment; a Long Rest alone does not remove them.\n\n"},{"file":"DescentReviewChanges/ReviewBible/docs/mechanics/GAMEPLAY_LOOP_CANON.md","sha256":"ba5ebcbdb77a27cb8fde88ba13fd05216d6808b10f7b58b64e6cba54eed62066","heading":"6. Route procedure","exactText":"## 6. Route procedure\n\nA route is written with five ratings:\n\n- **Length:** Number of Progress required.\n- **Risk:** Starting Pressure from 1–5.\n- **Mapping:** Unknown, Sketched, Mapped, or Secured.\n- **Capacity:** Maximum convoy Load that can pass without penalty.\n- **Sustenance:** Natural Supply available from successful Forage, rated 0–4. A route at Sustenance 0 cannot support routine Forage.\n\n### Progress per active Watch\n\n- Secured or Mapped route: 2 Progress.\n- Sketched route: 1 Progress.\n- Unknown or severely obstructed route: 0 Progress until Reconnaissance succeeds, then 1 Progress.\n\nA successful Reconnaissance activity may add +1 Progress or reduce Pressure by 1. A Critical Success can do both.\n\n**Forced March on an Unknown Route:** When the Expedition cannot or will not wait for Map Route, the Routekeeper may attempt a Severe route check. Success gains 1 Progress, increases Pressure by 2, and consumes one additional Supply per active character. Failure gains no Progress and causes a Standard complication. Critical Failure gains no Progress and causes a Severe complication. Forced March prevents a procedural deadlock, but Reconnaissance remains safer and more efficient.\n\nLoad above route Capacity reduces Progress by 1, minimum 0, and increases Pressure by 1.\n\n"},{"file":"DescentReviewChanges/ReviewBible/docs/mechanics/GAMEPLAY_LOOP_CANON.md","sha256":"ba5ebcbdb77a27cb8fde88ba13fd05216d6808b10f7b58b64e6cba54eed62066","heading":"7. Pressure and complications","exactText":"## 7. Pressure and complications\n\nPressure ranges from 0–6.\n\n| Pressure | State |\n|---:|---|\n| 0 | Secured; no routine complication roll |\n| 1 | Watched |\n| 2 | Unstable |\n| 3 | Hostile |\n| 4 | Hunted |\n| 5 | Overrun |\n| 6 | Immediate crisis |\n\nAt the end of each active Watch, roll 1d6. If the roll is equal to or lower than Pressure, resolve a complication.\n\nIncrease Pressure by 1 for each applicable condition:\n\n- Open combat or an explosion occurred during the Watch.\n- The convoy exceeds route Capacity.\n- An Attention-tagged cargo lot is first carried exposed into an active Watch, is revealed by a mishap, or loses its concealment. Continuous controlled carriage does not add Pressure again every Watch.\n- A member is missing, captured, or actively pursued.\n- The Expedition continued after an abort condition.\n- The camp is exposed or its location is known to enemies.\n\nReduce Pressure by 1 for each applicable condition, maximum 2 reductions per Watch:\n\n- Reconnaissance succeeds at Counter-Ambush or Conceal Trail.\n- The Expedition changes to a secured route.\n- The party spends a Watch lying low in a concealed camp.\n- A negotiated settlement ends pursuit.\n\nPressure cannot be reduced below the route's base Risk until the route is Secured.\n\n### Complication severity\n\n- Roll exceeds Pressure by exactly 0 because of a modifier or reroll: Minor.\n- Natural roll 2–6 that triggers: Standard.\n- Natural 1: Severe.\n- Pressure 6: Severe complication automatically; do not roll.\n\n"},{"file":"DescentReviewChanges/ReviewBible/docs/mechanics/GAMEPLAY_LOOP_CANON.md","sha256":"ba5ebcbdb77a27cb8fde88ba13fd05216d6808b10f7b58b64e6cba54eed62066","heading":"8. Team activities","exactText":"## 8. Team activities\n\nEach team chooses one activity per Watch. The listed ability or skill is a default, not the only possible approach.\n\n### Combat Team\n\n- **Advance:** Secure Progress through an occupied route. Athletics, Survival, or attack-based challenge.\n- **Breach:** Remove a defended obstacle. Athletics, tools, spellcasting, or siege procedure.\n- **Escort:** Protect Resource or Support personnel. On success, assistants avoid the first consequence suffered that Watch.\n- **Patrol:** Prevent incursions near camp. On success, reduce the severity of the next camp complication by one degree.\n\n### Reconnaissance Team\n\n- **Scout Ahead:** Reveal the next site, hazard, or encounter before commitment.\n- **Map Route:** Improve Mapping one step after accumulated successes equal to route Risk.\n- **Counter-Ambush:** Reduce Pressure by 1 on success.\n- **Conceal Trail:** Cancel one pursuit-based Pressure increase.\n\n### Research Team\n\n- **Identify:** Determine an item's function, risk, provenance, and Extraction Class.\n- **Translate:** Accumulate progress on a text, code, or inscription.\n- **Analyze Threat:** Learn one reliable resistance, vulnerability, behavior, or retreat trigger.\n- **Experiment:** Attempt a new formula, ritual, or interaction with explicit mishap stakes.\n\n### Resource Team\n\n- **Harvest:** Recover creature or environmental materials.\n- **Forage:** Produce Supply up to the route's Sustenance rating. Success yields Supply equal to Sustenance; Critical Success yields Sustenance plus half that amount, rounded up; Failure yields none; Critical Failure contaminates one Supply or increases Pressure by 1. Forage cannot produce more Supply than the route can sustainably provide during that Watch.\n- **Mine or Salvage:** Recover bulk materials and mechanical components.\n- **Preserve:** Prevent spoilage and reduce Fragile or Living cargo penalties.\n\n### Support Team\n\n- **Establish Camp:** Create or improve a camp.\n- **Treat:** Stabilize casualties, treat Wounds, and control disease or withdrawal.\n- **Repair:** Restore equipment Integrity and transport capacity.\n- **Transport:** Move, secure, redistribute, or hide cargo and casualties.\n\n### Team check procedure\n\n1. Select a lead character.\n2. Choose the approach and relevant skill.\n3. Add up to three trained assistants.\n4. Resolve against the route or tier DC.\n5. Apply the activity's outcome and any complication.\n\nA Critical Success normally grants the Success benefit plus one of: +1 Progress, -1 Pressure, one extra unit of output, or improved information. A Critical Failure normally costs time, Supply, Integrity, position, or secrecy in addition to failing.\n\n"},{"file":"DescentReviewChanges/ReviewBible/docs/mechanics/GAMEPLAY_LOOP_CANON.md","sha256":"ba5ebcbdb77a27cb8fde88ba13fd05216d6808b10f7b58b64e6cba54eed62066","heading":"9. Supply and attrition\n\n### Supply Units\n\nOne Supply Unit sustains one Medium character for one day and includes food, water, fuel, and ordinary camp consumables. Small characters still consume one unit; Large characters consume two.\n\nAt the end of each twenty-four hours, consume Supply equal to the Expedition's daily requirement.\n\nIf the Expedition cannot pay the full requirement, every unsupported character gains 1 Strain and cannot remove Strain that day.\n\n### Strain\n\nStrain represents exhaustion, sleep debt, hunger, exposure, and sustained magical stress.\n\n| Strain | Effect |\n|---:|---|\n| 0 | No penalty |\n| 1 | -1 initiative and expedition checks |\n| 2 | Speed -10 ft.; -1 attacks and saves |\n| 3 | Disadvantage on expedition checks; cannot Dash twice consecutively |\n| 4 | Maximum HP reduced by 25% |\n| 5 | Incapacitated except for slow travel and Rest |\n| 6 | Death or permanent collapse, as appropriate to the cause |\n\nA secure Long Rest removes 1 Strain. Load and cargo","exactText":"## 10. Load and cargo\n\n### Load Units\n\nOne Load is approximately ten pounds of ordinary cargo or one awkward bundle. Armor, weapons, packs, and carried treasure count unless an item states otherwise.\n\nA character's Carry Limit is equal to Strength score, modified by size:\n\n- Small: -2 Load.\n- Medium: no adjustment.\n- Large: double Carry Limit.\n\nAt more than half Carry Limit, the character is **Burdened**: Speed -10 ft. and disadvantage on checks to climb, swim, or move silently.\n\nA character cannot voluntarily travel while above Carry Limit.\n\n### Extraction Class\n\nExtraction Class measures handling danger, attention, and logistical complexity, not only weight.\n\n| Class | Example |\n|---:|---|\n| 0 | Ordinary trade goods |\n| 1 | Common monster parts or durable salvage |\n| 2 | Uncommon, fragile, or regulated cargo |\n| 3 | Rare artifact, volatile substance, or living captive |\n| 4 | Very rare strategic cargo or dangerous curse |\n| 5 | Epic cargo likely to attract sovereign or faction intervention |\n| 6 | Legendary documented-limit cargo requiring a dedicated operation |\n\nAdd one handling complication tag when applicable: Fragile, Volatile, Cursed, Living, Contraband, Perishable, or Attention.\n\n### Cargo priority\n\nBefore extraction, assign each cargo lot a priority from 1–5. When forced to abandon cargo, the lowest priority is abandoned first. Ties are decided by the Quartermaster or Expedition Leader.\n\n### Casualties and captives\n\nA Medium unconscious or restrained creature occupies 6 Load. A Small creature occupies 3 Load. A Large creature occupies 12 Load. Encounter loop","exactText":"## 12. Encounter loop\n\nAt the start of an encounter:\n\n1. Establish objective and stakes.\n2. Establish terrain, exits, cargo, civilians, and hazards.\n3. Determine surprise and starting distance.\n4. Roll initiative.\n5. Run turns until the objective is resolved, one side withdraws, or the scene changes mode.\n\n### Combat turn\n\n1. Resolve start-of-turn effects.\n2. Move and take Action/Bonus Action in any order permitted.\n3. Resolve end-of-turn saves and durations.\n4. Pass initiative.\n\n### Universal actions\n\nIn addition to class actions, characters can Attack, Dash, Disengage, Dodge, Help, Hide, Ready, Search, Use an Object, Guard, Withdraw, Secure Cargo, Disarm, Feint, Interpose, Overwatch, Pin, Press, Sunder, Trip, Silence the Caster, or Break Formation as defined in the Core Systems Bible.\n\n### Action-economy limits\n\n- **Shared Additional Action Slot:** A creature can receive no more than one additional full Action in a round, regardless of source. Effects granting a full Action do not stack; the creature chooses which one fills the slot. A specifically granted extra attack, movement, Bonus Action, or Reaction does not fill this slot unless the feature explicitly calls it an Action. An Action granted by a feature cannot retrigger that same feature.\n- A creature can take only one Reaction before its next turn unless a feature explicitly replaces, rather than adds to, that limit.\n- A character can maintain only one Stance unless a feature explicitly permits two.\n- **Independent actor cap:** A controller can command no more than two independent combat-capable actors with their own turns. Summons, autonomous deployables, independent reflections, companions, drones, and similar entities all count toward the same cap. Passive devices and minions that do not take full turns do not count. Additional minions act as one swarm, cohort, or unit stat block.\n- An actor without a command defends itself and takes no complex action.\n- **Dual Cast:** When a feature permits Dual Cast, it is one Action that resolves two Tier 1–2 spells with a casting time of one Action. Pay both RP costs. Only one of the two spells may require concentration. Dual Cast does not grant a second Action.\n- **Traumatic Casting:** A spell forced through the Weave by Traumatic Casting cannot refund, reset, or retrigger Traumatic Casting. The direct magical trauma caused by that use cannot be prevented or reduced by Traumatic Casting or by an effect created by the spell being forced through.\n- Cooldown resets cannot reset the same ability more than once per round.\n- A creature cannot lose more than one full turn to the same control source without a new save or escalation cost.\n\n### Retreat\n\nA side may declare Retreat at the start of a round. Retreat succeeds when the withdrawing side accumulates three Escape successes before suffering three Pursuit successes.\n\nEach round, one lead character makes an appropriate check. Allies can assist, create obstacles, carry casualties, or sacrifice cargo. The default round presents the Pursuit check and one meaningful threat, attack, hazard, or sacrifice—not the pursuer's complete nova or full multiattack routine. A failed check, Severe complication, or fictionally trapped position may justify greater danger.\n\nThe withdrawing side can gain at most one Escape success in a round from either the lead check or winning the round, unless a feature explicitly grants another. A Critical Success on the lead check may still grant two Escape successes. Losing a member, route, or vehicle can grant one Pursuit success.\n\nOn a successful Retreat, return to Expedition Mode with Pressure +1. On failure, the opposing side chooses the next encounter position or captures abandoned characters or cargo consistent with the fiction.\n\n"},{"file":"DescentReviewChanges/ReviewBible/docs/mechanics/GAMEPLAY_LOOP_CANON.md","sha256":"ba5ebcbdb77a27cb8fde88ba13fd05216d6808b10f7b58b64e6cba54eed62066","heading":"13. Downed, Wounds, and death","exactText":"## 13. Downed, Wounds, and death\n\nAt 0 HP, a character is Downed, Prone, and begins death saves at the end of each turn.\n\n- 10 or higher: one success.\n- 9 or lower: one failure.\n- Natural 20: regain 1 HP.\n- Natural 1: two failures.\n- Three successes: Stable.\n- Three failures: Dead.\n\nDamage while Downed causes one failure, or two if the damage is a critical hit. Healing ends Downed.\n\n### Wounds\n\nGain one Wound when any of the following occurs:\n\n- Dropped to 0 HP by a critical hit.\n- Suffered two death-save failures before stabilization.\n- Revived after death or magical soul separation.\n- Suffered a Severe hazard or injury result that specifies a Wound.\n\nEach Wound imposes -1 Constitution saves and reduces Hit Dice healing by 1 per die, minimum 1. At three Wounds, the character is Critical: Speed is halved and Strain cannot be removed until at least one Wound is treated.\n\nTreating a Wound requires a medical kit, one Supply, eight hours, and Medicine against the tier DC. Critical Success removes two Wounds; Success removes one; Failure consumes the Supply; Critical Failure also inflicts 1 Strain.\n\n"},{"file":"DescentReviewChanges/ReviewBible/docs/mechanics/GAMEPLAY_LOOP_CANON.md","sha256":"ba5ebcbdb77a27cb8fde88ba13fd05216d6808b10f7b58b64e6cba54eed62066","heading":"14. Encounter construction","exactText":"## 14. Encounter construction\n\nAssign every enemy a creature level. Challenge Rating may be used as creature level until a dedicated level is written.\n\n### Relative Threat Points\n\n| Creature relative to party level | Threat Points |\n|---:|---:|\n| -4 or lower | 5 |\n| -3 | 10 |\n| -2 | 15 |\n| -1 | 25 |\n| Equal | 40 |\n| +1 | 60 |\n| +2 | 90 |\n| +3 | 135 |\n| +4 or higher | 200 |\n\n### Four-character encounter budgets\n\n| Difficulty | Budget | Expected effect |\n|---|---:|---|\n| Routine | 50 | Low resource cost; failure unlikely |\n| Standard | 90 | Meaningful cost; normal expedition opposition |\n| Hard | 130 | Serious resource use; casualties possible |\n| Severe | 180 | Defeat possible without preparation |\n| Catastrophic | 240 | Intended only as a Gate, climax, or avoidable disaster |\n\nUse the following explicit multipliers; do not compound the 20% adjustment exponentially:\n\n| Party size | Budget multiplier |\n|---:|---:|\n| 3 | ×0.8 |\n| 4 | ×1.0 |\n| 5 | ×1.2 |\n| 6 | ×1.4 |\n\n### Difficulty accounting\n\nThe encounter's **final listed difficulty** includes creature Threat Points and all objective, hostile-terrain, surprise, and active-hazard adjustments. Apply each adjustment once. A worksheet that already labels an encounter Hard because it protects cargo does not step it to Severe merely because the cargo objective is written again.\n\n- Ordinary surprise is one step and normally changes position, advantage, or reactions.\n- A full unopposed enemy round is two steps and should require failed reconnaissance, a Severe complication, or equivalent fiction.\n- Defeat-or-drive-off encounters use creature budget as the main measure.\n- Cargo and hazard encounters add one step unless the final label already includes it.\n- Escort encounters require an explicit progress, survival, or movement clock; kill win rate alone does not measure them.\n- Retreat uses the three-Escape-before-three-Pursuit clock; kill-budget win rate does not determine its difficulty.\n\n### Calibration targets\n\n| Final label | Intended party success rate | Intended use |\n|---|---:|---|\n| Routine | 95–100% | Low resource cost; failure exceptional |\n| Standard | 70–90% | Meaningful cost; normal opposition |\n| Hard | 40–65% | Serious cost; casualties plausible |\n| Severe | 15–35% | Defeat likely without preparation or advantage |\n| Catastrophic | 0–10% | Gate, climax, or avoidable disaster |\n\nThe numeric budgets above remain a provisional chassis. V7.1 simulations showed Level 1 Standard encounters too lethal and Levels 5–16 Standard encounters too soft. Until a holdout calibration replaces the table, Level 1–2 Standard encounters should avoid pairs of above-level creatures, Levels 5–16 should use meaningful objectives and phases rather than arbitrary HP inflation, and Levels 17–20 require party-composition testing. Do not apply global class buffs or nerfs from proxy simulations.\n\n### Boss structure\n\nA boss encounter should include:\n\n- A visible objective beyond reducing HP.\n- At least one telegraphed ability.\n- At least two phases or a meaningful escalation.\n- Terrain or objects players can exploit.\n- A retreat or failure state.\n- Tenacity against repeated control.\n\n**Tenacity:** The first time each round a boss would become Stunned, Paralyzed, or lose its full turn, it instead becomes Staggered until the end of its next turn. A Gate Guardian may have two Tenacity uses per round.\n\n"},{"file":"DescentReviewChanges/ReviewBible/docs/mechanics/GAMEPLAY_LOOP_CANON.md","sha256":"ba5ebcbdb77a27cb8fde88ba13fd05216d6808b10f7b58b64e6cba54eed62066","heading":"15. Harvesting and salvage","exactText":"## 15. Harvesting and salvage\n\nAfter an encounter, the Resource Team may Harvest or Salvage during the current or next Watch.\n\n- A hurried attempt takes one hour and suffers disadvantage.\n- A normal attempt consumes one activity assignment.\n- Failure damages or contaminates one recoverable component.\n- Critical Failure may expose the team to poison, disease, curse, or Pressure.\n\nHarvested material becomes cargo immediately and must be preserved if Perishable.\n\n"},{"file":"DescentReviewChanges/ReviewBible/docs/mechanics/GAMEPLAY_LOOP_CANON.md","sha256":"ba5ebcbdb77a27cb8fde88ba13fd05216d6808b10f7b58b64e6cba54eed62066","heading":"16. Extraction","exactText":"## 16. Extraction\n\nExtraction begins when the Expedition formally turns toward the Haven or surface.\n\nBefore movement:\n\n1. Inventory all cargo, casualties, captives, vehicles, and Supply.\n2. Assign cargo priority.\n3. Select a route and abort alternatives.\n4. Determine convoy Load and route Capacity.\n5. Increase Pressure by the highest Extraction Class carried, divided by two and rounded down, maximum +3.\n\n### Extraction Test\n\nMake Extraction Tests according to Mapping and always after a Severe complication:\n\n| Mapping | Test cadence |\n|---|---|\n| Secured | At the route transition only |\n| Mapped | At the midpoint and exit when Length is 6 or more; otherwise at exit |\n| Sketched | Every 3 Progress and at exit |\n| Unknown | Every 2 Progress and at exit |\n\nA route transition is entry into a different named route, floor band, controlled territory, transport method, or chokepoint—not every ordinary room or Watch. Do not repeat a test at the same Progress unless a new Severe complication changes the convoy state.\n\nResolve each Extraction Test as follows:\n\n`d20 + Quartermaster's Survival, vehicle, or relevant tool modifier + assistants`\n\nDC equals the tier DC plus:\n\n- The highest Extraction Class carried: +0 through +6.\n- +1 once if any Attention cargo remains exposed.\n- +1 if the convoy is Burdened.\n- +1 for each Volatile, Cursed, or Living cargo lot not properly secured, maximum +3.\n- +2 if actively pursued.\n- -2 on a Secured route.\n\nThe total cargo-and-handling increase from Extraction Class, Attention, Burden, and unsecured dangerous lots is capped at +7 before pursuit and route modifiers.\n\nOutcomes:\n\n- **Critical Success:** No loss; reduce Pressure by 1 or gain +1 Progress.\n- **Success:** Cargo and convoy pass safely.\n- **Failure:** Lose eight hours and resolve a Standard complication, or place one low-priority cargo lot at risk.\n- **Critical Failure:** Resolve a Severe complication and place two low-priority cargo lots, one vehicle, or one casualty at risk.\n\nThe Director must state what is at risk before the players choose a response. On a Failure, the players choose between the stated time-and-complication cost and the stated low-priority cargo risk.\n\n"},{"file":"DescentReviewChanges/ReviewBible/docs/mechanics/GAMEPLAY_LOOP_CANON.md","sha256":"ba5ebcbdb77a27cb8fde88ba13fd05216d6808b10f7b58b64e6cba54eed62066","heading":"17. Haven resolution","exactText":"## 17. Haven resolution\n\nArrival at the Haven follows this order:\n\n**Tier I fast settlement:** When there is no disputed ownership, strategic cargo, active lien, quarantine crisis, contraband case, or faction hearing, resolve only Inspection → Appraisal → Sale → Sponsor Settlement → Recovery. Escalate to the full procedure whenever a skipped step becomes materially relevant.\n\n1. **Quarantine and inspection.** Identify disease, curse, contraband, and dangerous captives.\n2. **Provenance filing.** Record who found, financed, carried, and legally claims each major item.\n3. **Appraisal.** Determine function, authenticity, rarity, and base value.\n4. **Claims and liens.** Resolve sponsors, creditors, guild rights, sovereign seizure, and competing claimants.\n5. **Disposition.** Sell, auction, craft, research, store, ransom, liberate, return, or smuggle cargo.\n6. **Settlement.** Pay wages, shares, taxes, debts, insurance, and replacement costs.\n7. **Recovery.** Treat Wounds, remove Strain, repair gear, and replace personnel.\n8. **Standing.** Adjust faction reputation and Expedition renown.\n9. **Advancement.** Award XP and unlock training, property, specialists, and routes.\n10. **Next descent.** Set the next objective and reserve.\n\n### Appraisal\n\nUse Intelligence with Arcana, History, Nature, Religion, or the relevant craft proficiency against tier DC.\n\n- Critical Success: exact function, risks, provenance, and best current buyer.\n- Success: reliable function, rarity, and base value.\n- Failure: broad category and uncertain value.\n- Critical Failure: false assumption; Director records the error and reveals it when materially relevant.\n\n### Sale value\n\n`Final Value = Base Value × Provenance × Demand × Condition × Urgency`\n\nUse these multipliers:\n\n- Provenance: undocumented 0.5; disputed 0.75; documented 1.0; Gate-Sealed 1.25.\n- Demand: glutted 0.75; normal 1.0; sought 1.25; strategic 1.5.\n- Condition: damaged 0.5; worn 0.75; sound 1.0; exceptional 1.25.\n- Urgency: forced sale 0.75; normal 1.0; patient auction 1.1.\n\nRound to the nearest gold piece. Taxes, shares, and liens are paid after value is determined.\n\n### Haven tax schedule\n\n| Cargo or transaction | Tax |\n|---|---:|\n| Registered routine goods | 3% |\n| Licensed crafting materials or crafted goods | 5% |\n| Artifacts and auctioned relics | 6% |\n| Strategic or regulated cargo | 8% |\n| Authorized contraband amnesty, surrender, or disposal | 12% |\n\nApply one category, normally the highest applicable rate. A legal exemption, faction privilege, or negotiated charter must be written before the sale.\n\n### Coin assay and reminting\n\nIron Ledger assay houses use Investigation, relevant tools, or mercantile proficiency. Standard DCs are 10 for obvious shaving, 13 for professional clipping or mixed weight, 16 for plated or sophisticated counterfeit coins, and 19 for a masterwork false die or mint. Assay costs 1% of the lot's face value, minimum 1 gp. Reminting costs 2% of recovered metal value. The holder receives the actual legitimate metal value minus fees.\n\nConfirmed counterfeit coin or plated false currency is seized in full. Genuine coins or legitimate metal in the same documented lot are returned after fees. An innocent holder with documented provenance pays no criminal fine. A knowing holder pays the greater of 10% of the false lot's face value or the assay and remint fees.\n\n### Public commodity index\n\nThese are common Haven baselines before demand, condition, provenance, and urgency multipliers:\n\n| Documented source band | Typical lot range | Median reference |\n|---|---:|---:|\n| Tier I | 5–15 gp | 10 gp |\n| Tier II | 15–50 gp | 30 gp |\n| Tier III | 51–148 gp | 100 gp |\n| Tier IV | 151–499 gp | 320 gp |\n| Tier V | 512–1,465 gp | 950 gp |\n\nThe values describe individual documented material lots, not finished artifacts. Directors should alter Demand rather than rewriting Base Value for ordinary market fluctuation.\n\n### Underways Heat\n\nIllegal work in the Underways creates Heat from 0–6. Petty illicit trade adds 1 Heat, restricted cargo adds 2, strategic theft or smuggling adds 3, and trafficking or forbidden cargo adds 4. At the end of each Haven Turn involving the operation, roll 1d6; a result equal to or below Heat creates an enforcement complication. A bribe equal to 10% of the lot's value reduces Heat by 1; 25% reduces it by 2. A bribe cannot be paid at Heat 0. The floor of Heat 1 applies only when the operation had Heat 1 or higher before the bribe and remains active. Capture normally causes seizure and a faction-standing loss in addition to legal consequences.\n\n### Labor, bondage, rescue, and trafficking\n\nTrack each person's legal and factual status separately: **Free, Contracted, Debt-Bound, Penal, Enslaved, Rescued,** or **Disputed**. Do not assign universal prices by race or ancestry. Legal wages are normally 1 gp per day for unskilled work, 3 gp for skilled work, and 10 gp for a specialist; dangerous expedition work doubles the rate. A lawful debt bond records principal plus no more than 10% interest per Opening, minus credited wages. Coercion, kidnapping, fraud, or unlawful confinement voids any buyout requirement.\n\nInvestigating trafficking uses an Evidence Clock from 0–4. Success adds 1 Evidence and Critical Success adds 2. At 4 Evidence, the investigators can trigger a warrant, raid, public hearing, rescue operation, or faction confrontation. Consensual adult sex work is not trafficking and is resolved through patronage, licensing, safety, information, and reputation procedures.\n\n### Low Lantern and Draughtworks activities\n\nDuring one Haven Turn a character may:\n\n- **Seek Patronage or Information:** Pay an appropriate lifestyle cost and make a social check; Success gains a contact, lead, or audience, while Critical Failure creates rumor, blackmail, or unwanted attention.\n- **Secure a License:** Pay the district fee and satisfy the relevant guild or health check to operate legally.\n- **Brew a Batch:** Use the crafting formula, ingredients, facility, and one day of work; illegal substances also create Underways Heat.\n- **Treat Dependence or Withdrawal:** Spend one day under qualified care to gain advantage on the next recovery check and prevent one ordinary withdrawal escalation.\n- **Protect Workers:** Establish guards, safe transport, debt review, medical access, or an evidence operation; Success reduces exploitation risk or adds Evidence against a trafficker.\n\n### Expedition cargo insurance\n\nInsurance must be purchased and the cargo declared before loss.\n\n| Cargo category | Premium |\n|---|---:|\n| Ordinary | 5% of insured value |\n| Dangerous, volatile, cursed, or living | 10% |\n| Attention or strategic | 15% |\n\nThe deductible is 20% of insured value. Maximum payout rates are 60% for undocumented provenance, 75% for disputed provenance, and 90% for documented or Gate-Sealed provenance.\n\n`Payout = insured value × provenance payout rate - deductible`\n\nPayout cannot exceed insured value. Covered losses include a recorded extraction complication, theft, transport failure, or named insured hazard. Fraud, undeclared cargo, voluntary abandonment outside an insured-sacrifice clause, and loss after knowingly ignoring an abort condition are excluded.\n\n### Sponsor shares\n\nDefault agreements:\n\n| Sponsor | Up-front support | Share of net expedition proceeds |\n|---|---|---:|\n| Self-funded | None beyond own capital | 0% |\n| Local guild | Supplies and ordinary licenses | 20% |\n| Merchant or bank | Capital, transport, appraisal access | 30% |\n| Noble or temple | Capital, political cover, specialists | 35% |\n| Sovereign or great institution | Full logistical support and legal force | 45% |\n\nSpecific contracts may differ. Net proceeds means sale income after direct Haven taxes and mandatory rescue or quarantine costs, but before personal wages and discretionary purchases.\n\nEvery charter records three different thresholds:\n\n- **Gross cargo target:** Appraised sale value before taxes and shares.\n- **Net expedition target:** Value after mandatory Haven costs and sponsor share.\n- **True profit target:** Value remaining after wages, repairs, replacement equipment, debt service, and required reserves.\n\nThe charter designates one as the primary success metric. Never call an expedition profitable without stating which threshold is being used.\n\n"},{"file":"DescentReviewChanges/ReviewBible/docs/mechanics/GAMEPLAY_LOOP_CANON.md","sha256":"ba5ebcbdb77a27cb8fde88ba13fd05216d6808b10f7b58b64e6cba54eed62066","heading":"18. Reputation and renown","exactText":"## 18. Reputation and renown\n\nUse a standing score from -5 to +5 with each faction.\n\n| Score | Standing |\n|---:|---|\n| -5 | Kill-on-sight or total proscription |\n| -4 | Hunted |\n| -3 | Hostile |\n| -2 | Distrusted |\n| -1 | Unwelcome |\n| 0 | Unknown or neutral |\n| +1 | Recognized |\n| +2 | Favored |\n| +3 | Trusted |\n| +4 | Champion |\n| +5 | Institutional authority |\n\nStanding also controls routine institutional access:\n\n| Score | Default access |\n|---:|---|\n| -5 | Proscribed; detention or attack expected |\n| -4 | Hunted; covert contacts only |\n| -3 | Hostile; enemy or black-market intermediaries only |\n| -2 | Necessities at surcharge; no licenses, credit, or restricted auctions |\n| -1 | Routine public market only |\n| 0 | Ordinary markets, lodging, and contracts |\n| +1 | Ordinary licenses and verified-buyer access |\n| +2 | Restricted-material desks and selected auctions |\n| +3 | Institutional credit, bonded storage, and sensitive contracts |\n| +4 | Strategic auctions, command audiences, and major exemptions |\n| +5 | Contract-issuing and institutional authority |\n\nA normal success affecting a faction changes standing by 1. A major public success or betrayal changes it by 2. Standing cannot change by more than 2 from one scene without a campaign-defining event.\n\nExpedition renown uses the established cultural scale:\n\n- Survive Tier I: Proven adventurer.\n- Reach Tier II: Established professional.\n- Reach the Haven profitably: Career-defining success.\n- Operate through Tier III: Elite expedition member.\n- Return from Tier IV: Famous or feared.\n- Return from Tier V: Living legend.\n- Face the Sixth Gate: Historical figure.\n- Pass the Sixth Gate: No verified public record.\n\n"},{"file":"DescentReviewChanges/ReviewBible/docs/mechanics/GAMEPLAY_LOOP_CANON.md","sha256":"ba5ebcbdb77a27cb8fde88ba13fd05216d6808b10f7b58b64e6cba54eed62066","heading":"19. Advancement rewards","exactText":"## 19. Advancement rewards\n\n**Milestone advancement is the default for Opening campaigns.** Award a level when the Expedition completes a meaningful campaign milestone such as establishing a secured route, completing a major contract, resolving a Gate-scale objective, changing Haven institutions, or surviving a defining extraction.\n\nThe detailed XP procedure below is optional for tables that want granular progression. Do not combine milestone levels and detailed XP awards during the same advancement period.\n\nWhen using detailed XP, award it to every Expedition member who materially contributed during the relevant Watch, not only characters who entered combat.\n\nUse the gap between the character's current level and next level as the basis:\n\n| Achievement | XP per character |\n|---|---:|\n| Routine encounter or activity | 5% of level gap |\n| Standard encounter or objective | 10% |\n| Hard encounter or major discovery | 15% |\n| Severe encounter or expedition objective | 25% |\n| Gate Guardian victory | 40% |\n| Profitable extraction meeting the declared threshold | 10% |\n| First verified route, major text, or faction breakthrough | 5–15% |\n\nRound to the nearest 10 XP. Do not award more than 60% of a level gap from one scene. Combat, research, resource, support, and reconnaissance achievements are valued by difficulty and consequence, not by body count.\n\nNon-XP rewards include routes, licenses, property, faction authority, specialists, formulas, claims, camps, and institutional changes.\n\n"},{"file":"DescentReviewChanges/ReviewBible/docs/mechanics/GAMEPLAY_LOOP_CANON.md","sha256":"ba5ebcbdb77a27cb8fde88ba13fd05216d6808b10f7b58b64e6cba54eed62066","heading":"21. Primary source locations\n\n| Layer | Location | What it holds |\n|-------|----------|---------------|\n| **Player Rulebook (source of truth)** | `decennium descent player rulebook.docx` · extract `rulebook_output.txt` § **Expeditions in Aethel** → **The 12-Person Expedition Structure** (lines **3801–3926**) | Full RAW: teams, periods, skill checks, examples, base camps, advancement, conclusion — **authoritative** |\n| **Current extract** | `rulebook_output.txt` lines **3802–3926** | Current Player Rulebook extract |\n| **Companion JSON (mirror only)** | `src/data/dungeon-mechanics.json` → `expedition` | `maxSize: 12`, five `teams[]` with size bands + one-line `function`, `timePeriods[]` — **subset mirror**, not a second authority |\n| **Companion UI (read-only display)** | `src/components/DungeonGuide.tsx` — Expedition card | Renders JSON teams; no assignment or period scheduling |\n| **Structured-data maintenance** | `src/data/dungeon-mechanics.json` | Maintained as an implementation mirror; there is currently no automatic rulebook-to-expedition parser |\n\n**Encoding note:** `rulebook_output.txt` is a UTF-8 searchable mirror of the current Player Rulebook.\n\n---\n\n## 2. RAW summary (with citations)\n\n### 2.1 Definition — organizational cap, not combat cap\n\n> In Decennium Descent, adventuring parties are known as **Expeditions** and can consist of **up to 12 characters**. This limit represents the **total number of individuals working together toward common goals, not just those engaged in combat**. An effective Expedition distributes its members across various activities to maximize efficiency, resource gathering, information collection, and overall success.\n\n— `rulebook_output.txt` **3804–3805** (also `rulebook_output.txt` **3804–3805**)\n\n### 2.2 Five activity teams (size bands)\n\n| Team | Optimal size | Function (RAW) |\n|------|--------------|----------------|\n| **Combat Team** | 4–6 | Battles, dungeon delving, immediate threats |\n| **Reconnaissance** | 1–3 | Scouting ahead, gathering intelligence, mapping terrain |\n| **Research** | 1–3 | Studying artifacts, researching lore, identifying items, translating texts |\n| **Resource** | 2–4 | Hunting, gathering, cooking, crafting, harvesting materials |\n| **Support** | 1–3 | Camp management, equipment maintenance, healing, transportation |\n\nKey roles, skill considerations, and alignment notes for Combat are in the rulebook block (**3808–3832**). JSON alignment: `dungeon-mechanics.json` `expedition.teams[]` matches names, size bands, and shortened functions.\n\n### 2.3 Time periods and assignment\n\n> Each expedition day is divided into three periods: **morning, afternoon, and evening**  \n> Characters can be assigned to different activities during each period  \n> Some activities require multiple time periods to complete (e.g., deep research, long-distance scouting)\n\n— `rulebook_output.txt` **3834–3837**\n\n**Skill checks:** activities require appropriate skill checks; proficiency grants advantages; specialized tools may be required or grant bonuses (**3838–3841**).\n\n**Activity results (benefits per team):** Combat → advancement, treasure, reputation, monster parts; Reconnaissance → maps, information, future encounter advantages; Research → knowledge, spell discoveries, historical context; Resource → food, materials, crafting components; Support → extended expedition duration, equipment bonuses, recovery (**3842–3848**).\n\n### 2.4 Worked examples (rulebook)\n\nThree full allocation examples — **Dungeon Exploration**, **Wilderness Expedition**, **Urban Investigation** — with 12-seat breakdowns including NPC specialists (**3849–3882**). Use these as RAW templates, not mandatory loadouts.\n\n### 2.5 Benefits of role distribution\n\n1. Resource efficiency — parallel gathering while combat advances  \n2. Risk management — not all members exposed to the same dangers  \n3. Downtime productivity — injured PCs contribute off-combat  \n4. Narrative depth — non-combat engagement with the world  \n\n— `rulebook_output.txt` **3883–3895**\n\n### 2.6 Managing non-combat activities — resolution methods\n\n| Method | Use |\n|--------|-----|\n| **Quick Resolution** | Simple skill checks with predetermined outcomes |\n| **Scene-Based** | Brief RP scenes with key decision points |\n| **Parallel Play** | Split session focus across teams |\n| **Between-Session** | Resolve off-clock activities between sessions |\n\n**Spotlight:** rotate detailed attention; use montages; players may control multiple characters across teams (**3896–3905**).\n\n### 2.7 Expedition base camps\n\nOperational centers with functions: safe rest, supply storage, workshop, team coordination (**3906–3912**).\n\n**Types:** Temporary Camp · Fortified Outpost · Research Station · Forward Base (**3913–3917**).\n\n### 2.8 Expedition advancement\n\nReputation, equipment/tools, base improvements, NPC specialist allies, logistics network (**3918–3923**).\n\n### 2.9 Conclusion (organizational vs combat party)\n\n> The **12-person expedition limit** in Decennium Descent represents a **complex organizational structure rather than simply a combat party size**. By strategically assigning characters to different activities, players can maximize their effectiveness, gather valuable resources, and create a more immersive and realistic adventuring experience. This system encourages creative problem-solving, teamwork, and engagement with all aspects of the game world **beyond combat**.\n\n— `rulebook_output.txt` **3925–3926**\n\n---\n\n## 3. It is not a morality score and it is not a hidden multiplier on the laws of magic.\n\n## Scale\n\nFaction standing is stored as `{ [factionId]: score }` on the character save, normally from **−3 (Hated)** through **+3 (Honored)**. Backgrounds and play can set or change starting standing.\n\n## What reputation can change\n\nA faction may change:\n\n- prices, credit, insurance, salvage rights, and expedition sponsorship;\n- access to restricted libraries, teachers, rituals, recipes, laboratories, temples, circles, and sanctuaries;\n- information quality, contacts, safe houses, legal treatment, hostility, and political support;\n- availability of a **named** focus, training feature, consumable, or scene benefit whose rules are written explicitly.\n\n## What reputation does not change automatically\n\nReputation does **not** silently modify:\n\n- a spell's Resonance Rank;\n- base RP cost;\n- natural maximum RP;\n- Dissonance gained from a cast;\n- the Frayed / Discordant / Fractured thresholds;\n- a character's moral alignment.\n\nIf a guild teaches a cheaper technique or a shrine grants a recovery boon, that is an explicit earned benefit, not proof that the Weave itself favors members of that institution.\n\n## Magic connection\n\nThe universal magic rules are in [`RESONANCE_CANON.md`](./RESONANCE_CANON.md). Faction standing can provide **tools for dealing with magic**, but never rewrites the underlying resonance law merely because the character is liked or hated.\n\n## Companion implementation\n\n- `src/data/reputation.js` owns faction definitions and starting standing.\n- `character-storage.js` persists the reputation map.\n- `resonance-economy.js` retains compatibility helper names for old saves/code, but automatic reputation-to-RP and reputation-to-spell-cost modifiers return **0** under current canon.\n\nThis separation is deliberate: institutions matter socially and materially; Resonance/Dissonance remain metaphysical rules.\n"},{"file":"DescentReviewChanges/ReviewBible/docs/mechanics/STRAIN_PLAYTEST.md","sha256":"32ca16894c29ba2906f8d510ef9781951bc8780e4a91ee84671f250f4de62b5f","heading":"entire guide","exactText":"# Strain — source-qualified playtest reference\n\n**Status: REVIEWED / reference-VALIDATED within the supplied namespace, penalty and recovery scope.** [GPT's exact disposition](../../../../docs/audit/2026-10-08/full-game/GPT_STRAIN_FINAL_REVIEW.md) confirms this bounded candidate. A Dash during its current turn excludes that current end, using the exact marker-exclusion rule from combat. Thus turn1 Dash blocks another that turn and turn2; turn2's end unlocks turn3. An off-turn Dash locks through the next regular turn's end. Extra turns cannot substitute for that marker.\n\nOutside initiative, unlock at Dash time+6 seconds. Detailed stacking with other travel-pace modifiers remains the travel review's responsibility.\n\nBrief communication is possible if no other condition prevents it. This creates no free administration or universal restraint/capture rule. Those limits apply to serum recovery too, including non-Warrior recipients. Exact completion timestamps and IDs prevent replay. Work, travel, short rest, sleep and recreation cannot spend the same active hours twice. Unfinished/interrupted activity provides no completion benefit.\n\n**Rest Debt order.** First complete a qualifying safe8hRestWatch and clear debt it satisfies. Then evaluate beneficial Long Rest eligibility. If eligible, apply its sourced benefits, including serum reset and one eligible expedition point when secure. Persistent food/water/source blocks still prevent expedition reduction. An additional safe RestWatch inside the24h reward cap can discharge debt but cannot restore resources, remove expedition Strain, reset the beneficial-rest clock or reset short-rest counts.\n\n**Secure Long Rest.** Remove one eligible expedition point chosen by the patient, once the rest qualifies and no reduction block remains. This is a separate benefit from serum reset, so one actual qualifying rest may do both. Class-only serum reduction never silently removes expedition points.\n\n**Morale Rest — NEW endpoint.** Four consecutive safe hours of voluntarily chosen recreation, without forced substance use, work, expedition travel or simultaneous sleep/ShortRest. At most one beneficial Morale Rest per24 elapsed hours. Remove one eligible expedition point. It never removes a Wound. A distinct4hMoraleRest plus an eligible8hsecureLongRest can legitimately remove two points; there is no new shared one-point cap.\n\n**Haven care.** One full day means24 consecutive hours under qualifying Haven care/location conditions, with real care, food and water supplied under campaign rules. One active care interval per patient; interrupted care gives no completion benefit and restarting requires a fresh24h window. Incompatible practitioner activities cannot be double-counted.\n\nThe Strain reference does not replace HP/RP/RC rest rewards or settle all supply prices, travel limits or camp security. It resolves specific conflicts between approved Core V2, compatible Player Rulebook V6 rules and the retained Gameplay Loop. Original books and alternate procedures remain unchanged. Full conditions, equipment, hazards and recovery integration remain separate work.\n\nUse with [class rules](CLASS_PLAYTEST_RULES.md), [spell rules](SPELL_INTEGRATION_RULES.md) and the [complete coverage ledger](GAME_DEVELOPMENT_COVERAGE.md). The [source conference](../../../../docs/audit/2026-10-08/full-game/CODEX_CORE_RESOLUTION_CONFERENCE.json), [V6 findings](../../../../docs/audit/2026-10-08/full-game/CODEX_CORE_SOURCE_FOLLOWUP.md) and [GPT proposal](../../../../docs/audit/2026-10-08/full-game/GPT_CORE_STATE_AND_INJURY_PROPOSAL.md) preserve the reasoning.\n\n## Which rule applies\n\nThe domain authority is [Core Systems Known Descent V2](../../../../books/player/Decennium_Descent_Core_Systems_Bible_v2_Known_Descent.docx). The approved [Player Rulebook V6](../../../../books/player/Decennium_Descent_Player_Rulebook_v6.docx) supplies compatible rules that Core does not replace. This overlay states the selected interpretation or newly authored rule wherever those sources leave a gap. An explicit exception on an operative, reviewed class/spell/item card applies only within its printed scope; a historical alternate or a same-named feature is not an extra grant. This candidate does not promote an entire historical branch.\n\n| Question | Active rule and provenance | Inactive alternate |\n|---|---|---|\n| Attacks | Core V2: meet AC; natural 1 misses; natural 20 hits and doubles damage dice. | Applying the general Gameplay four-degree roll to attacks. |\n| Task checks | Explicitly adopted Gameplay four degrees and natural shifts. | Assuming every critical result creates an unprinted effect. |\n| Ordinary saves | Binary meet-DC test; printed failure margins remain literal. Natural 1/20 do not generically shift saves in this candidate. This separation is an integration choice. | Automatic critical saves or natural-result shifts inferred from the task rule. |\n| Death-save timing | Core and V6: start of turn; regular-turn-only timing is a provisional guard. | Gameplay end-of-turn saves. |\n| Wounds | Core trigger/save, Constitution-save and Hit-Dice-recovery penalties, and three-Wound Critical state. | Gameplay automatic Wound triggers, Hit Dice healing penalty, half Speed and Strain lock. |\n| Named Wounded condition | Core paragraph 782 expressly describes the same Wound count, Constitution penalty, Hit Dice recovery and three-Wound Critical state. Apply those records once. | V6 Appendix A paragraphs 9971–9974 give a conflicting temporary condition cleared by magic/rest with Constitution-save disadvantage; Core replaces it here. |\n| Stable, instant death, knockout and temporary HP | Compatible recovered V6 rules. | Treating these recovered rules as newly invented. |\n| Ordinary Wound treatment | Explicit Gameplay-derived integration below. | Claiming it is original Core wording or ordinary HP healing. |\n| Injury table and unresolved thresholds | New provisional choices below, with their search limits stated. | Dropping Core's failure-by-5 consequence because its table was not located. |\n\n## Rolls\n\nChoose the roll type before rolling. Use the actual ability modifier, including negative values, and normal proficiency only when applicable. Existing advantage/disadvantage chooses the higher/lower of two d20s; multiple sources do not stack, and any advantage plus disadvantage cancels. Add other legal modifiers once. Fractions round down unless a rule specifies otherwise. One tile is 5 feet. Class L, S and effect M retain their definitions in the shared class rules.\n\n**Attack:** roll d20 plus the appropriate attack modifier against AC. Meeting AC hits. Natural 1 always misses; natural 20 always hits and critically doubles damage dice, not flat modifiers. Beating AC by 10 is not a critical hit. An explicit broader critical range does not stack with another; use the broadest eligible range. In this overlay, a natural result below 20 in that range must still meet AC unless the feature expressly says it always hits.\n\n**Task, skill or tool check against a fixed DC:** total at least DC+10 is Critical Success; at least DC is Success; at most DC-10 is Critical Failure; other totals below DC are Failure. Natural 20 then improves one degree and natural 1 worsens one, bounded at either end. A task printing only success/failure collapses the two upper degrees to success and two lower degrees to failure; no extra consequence is invented. A natural 20 can still fail an otherwise impossible check, and a natural 1 with sufficient modifiers can still succeed.\n\n**Saving throw:** total at least DC succeeds; otherwise it fails. Natural 1 and 20 have no generic special outcome. A printed failure by 5 or 10 compares the final total with the DC; it does not add an unprinted critical-save system. Death saves are the specific exception below.\n\n**Opposed check, new provisional tie rule:** compare final totals. Higher wins; an exact tie leaves the existing state unchanged, so the initiating creature fails to change it. Do not turn task natural shifts into hidden numeric modifiers. A maneuver explicitly printing degree outcomes must specify how those apply.\n\n## Resolve a damage event\n\nResolve each genuinely separate hit or damage event once. Do not split one packet to multiply Wound saves or combine separate hits merely to cross a threshold. Feature-specific prevention that acts before damage or before reaching zero resolves at its printed timing first.\n\n1. Resolve attack/save, damage and applicable defenses. The remaining nonnegative amount is **resolved damage**.\n2. Record HP, Wounds and zero-HP state before the event. Remove temporary HP first. **Post-temp damage = resolved damage minus temporary HP absorbed.** Keep this amount even when it exceeds current HP; floor the resulting current HP at zero.\n3. Check V6 Instant Death. If post-temp damage minus HP before the hit is at least maximum HP, the creature dies. For a creature already at zero, this is post-temp damage at least maximum HP. Stop: there is no Wound save, death-save failure or successful knockout from that event.\n4. Otherwise, a living creature makes one Wound save if it dropped from positive HP to zero, if **2 × post-temp damage is at least maximum HP**, or if the effect actually inflicts Maiming. Overlapping triggers still cause only one save. The post-temp threshold and one-save rule are new provisional interpretations. Maiming is an independent trigger even when temporary HP absorbs all ordinary damage; immunity to Maiming itself still applies.\n5. Make a Constitution save against **max(10, floor(post-temp damage / 2))**. Use pre-event Wounds for the -1-per-Wound penalty. Maiming with zero post-temp damage uses DC 10. A Wound gained now cannot penalize the roll that created it.\n6. A success adds no Wound. A failure that began with zero, one or two Wounds adds exactly one; failure by 5 or more also gives that Wound one Injury. A failure that began with three Wounds kills the creature instead: no fourth Wound and no Injury roll. Stop processing later living-target consequences if dead.\n7. If the creature was already at zero before this event and post-temp damage is positive, end Stable. A creature using death saves adds one failure, or two for a critical hit; three failures kill it. An ordinary creature that does not use death saves dies instead, without starting death counters. A hit from positive HP to zero does not also cause a same-packet death failure.\n8. If this event newly reduces positive HP to zero, a valid melee knockout makes the surviving creature Stable. Otherwise a character/significant NPC using death saves begins dying; an ordinary monster dies. A previously Stable creature whose temporary HP absorbed all damage remains Stable unless an independent Maiming result killed it. The Director identifies significant NPCs consistently. Knockout cannot override Instant Death or a fatal Wound save.\n\nCore's Maiming and Wound trigger rules remain subject to an actual feature's eligibility. The reference helper receives already legal events; it does not grant every attack Maiming or a damage-prevention exception.\n\n## Zero HP and recovery\n\nA living character at zero HP is Unconscious. Core's condition already includes Incapacitated, unawareness, dropping held items and falling Prone; an adjacent hit is critical as specified there. Healing does not silently remove other independent causes of Unconscious or Prone.\n\nWhile dying, make one death save at the **start of each regular initiative turn**. Roll d20 without modifiers unless a feature specifically modifies death saves. Natural 20 restores 1 HP; natural 1 adds two failures. Other results of 10 or more add one success; 9 or less add one failure. Three successes make the creature Stable; three failures make it Dead. Extra turns and Actions add no extra death saves. Stable or Dead creatures do not roll them.\n\nRegaining positive HP or becoming Stable resets both counters. Temporary HP does not raise normal HP, end Unconscious or reset counters. Fully absorbed damage does not end Stable or add a death failure in this provisional interpretation. Independent Maiming can still cause its Wound save.\n\n**Stabilize:** an Action and DC 10 Wisdom (Medicine) check, or an Action using one healer's-kit use automatically, stabilizes a living dying creature at zero. It remains Unconscious and makes no death saves. Roll 1d4 once when it becomes Stable and record its absolute recovery time that many hours later. If still Stable then, it regains 1 HP. Damage reaching normal HP cancels that deadline and resumes dying with the appropriate failures. Rest and Stable recovery use the same elapsed clock, so four hours of recovery inside an eight-hour Watch do not cost twelve hours. A later rest does not separately replay the Stable healing event.\n\n**Dead:** ordinary healing and stabilization do not restore life. Resurrection must satisfy its own willing-soul, body, time, cost and target rules. It does not automatically remove Wounds, Corruption, debts, legal death or claims upon the body. Corpses cannot supply repeated resource triggers.\n\n## Wounds and Critical state\n\nEach Wound imposes **-1 Constitution saves** and reduces the normal maximum number of Hit Dice recovered per long rest by one, to a minimum of zero. It does not reduce each healing die. The broader recovery rules own the normal recovery maximum and rest legality.\n\nAt three Wounds the creature is **Critically Wounded**: Speed is reduced by 10 feet, to minimum zero, and the next failed Wound save is fatal. The failure that first raises two Wounds to three is not that later failure. Core's named **Wounded** condition (paragraph 782, preserved in [the 28-condition source register](../../data/playtest/core_condition_sources.json)) explicitly summarizes these same Wounds; it does not add duplicate penalties. Its conflicting V6 predecessor is inactive in this overlay. This individual source comparison does not complete the remaining condition audit.\n\n**New provisional healing ceiling:** while Critical, healing cannot increase HP above **max(1, maximum HP - 1)**. Maximum HP itself does not change. Gaining the third Wound does not immediately remove existing HP; a later attempted heal also cannot reduce HP. The minimum of one permits recovery when maximum HP is one and is an explicit exception to “cannot regain full HP.” Removing enough Wounds to leave Critical removes its Speed penalty and ceiling, but does not itself heal HP.\n\n## Linked injuries\n\nThe inspected eight approved books and current text sources reference an Injury table, but no actual table was located. The full Wound/Medicine searches of both Core editions found no alternate general treatment procedure. Those are search limits, not proof about every archive. This **new provisional d6 table** supplies Core's failure-by-5 consequence until a reviewed replacement is adopted.\n\n| d6 | Injury | Exact penalty |\n|---:|---|---|\n| 1 | Concussion | -1 Initiative and Perception checks. |\n| 2 | Offensive Trauma | -1 attack rolls. |\n| 3 | Mobility Trauma | All non-teleport Speeds reduced by 5 feet, minimum zero. |\n| 4 | Fine-Motor Trauma | -1 tool checks and Dexterity checks primarily manipulating an object, mechanism, lock or device. |\n| 5 | Exertion Trauma | -1 Strength and Dexterity ability checks. |\n| 6 | Cognitive Trauma | -1 Intelligence, Wisdom and Charisma ability checks. |\n\nLink each result to the one Wound just gained. An Injury never adds another Wound. Record duplicate results separately, but identical penalties apply once. Different Injury penalties affecting the same roll or stat also use only the strongest Injury penalty. V6 paragraph 7925 explicitly calls Initiative a Dexterity check, so both Concussion and Exertion Trauma can apply, with only one -1 Injury penalty. An injury affects future Initiative rolls, never an order already established. This does not change other Initiative procedures. Mobility Trauma can coexist with the separate Core Critical Speed penalty, applying both once. Other effects keep their own stacking rules.\n\nRemoving a Wound ends its linked Injury. A duplicate penalty persists while any remaining Wound carries that result. When a removal effect names no specific Wound, the patient chooses existing records. Injuries cause no extra damage, Actions lost, disadvantage, maximum-HP reduction or resource triggers. Describe equivalent trauma for unusual anatomy while retaining the printed penalty.\n\n## Ordinary Wound treatment\n\nThis is an explicit Gameplay-derived integration. A living patient with at least one Wound receives eight elapsed hours of treatment with a medical kit and **one Supply**. **New provisional cost decision:** commit and consume that Supply once when treatment begins, on every attempt, even on success or interruption; no extra Supply is charged for failure. The retained Gameplay text requires one Supply and explicitly mentions consumption on failure but does not clearly settle success refunds. This candidate deliberately settles the ambiguity as a cost on every attempt, rather than claiming that interpretation is recovered source wording. Choose the Wound being treated before beginning. At completion roll Wisdom (Medicine) against the applicable tier DC: I 12, II 13, III 15, IV 16, V 18, VI 19. Unverified higher-tier procedures remain with the later Director/hazard pass.\n\nCritical Success removes the chosen Wound and one other existing Wound chosen by the patient, if available. Success removes the chosen Wound. Failure removes none. Critical Failure removes none and gives the **patient** one Strain; identifying the patient is a new clarification. Associated Injuries end with removed Wounds. Treatment itself does not restore HP. Whether treatment qualifies as a legal rest is determined separately; the active healer cannot automatically claim an eight-hour Rest Watch while treating. Count the eight hours once, with any Stable recovery occurring on the same clock.\n\n## Verification scope\n\n`tools/core_playtest_rules.py` is a reference for roll types and the damage/Wound/death state transitions. It does not select legal Actions or targets, run all class reactions, roll damage, apply every defense, adjudicate all conditions, run medical-kit/Supply inventories or simulate a campaign clock. Its callers supply legal events and actual elapsed time. `tools/validate_core_playtests.py` reports named regression cases and sampled state invariants. A specific reviewed feature retains its printed costs, prerequisites and exceptions.\n\n## Source decisions\n\nThe [exact packet](../../../../docs/audit/2026-10-08/full-game/COMBAT_ACTION_VISION_SOURCE_PACKET.json) contains 217 paragraphs from unchanged Core V2 and V6 books. Paragraph indices are zero-based all Word XML `w:p` descendants. [Source validation](COMBAT_SOURCE_VALIDATION.json) verifies quotations and whole-book hashes.
