The [first GPT proposal](../../../../docs/audit/2026-10-08/full-game/GPT_COMBAT_ACTION_TIMING_FIRST_REVIEW.md), [counterexamples](../../../../docs/audit/2026-10-08/full-game/CODEX_COMBAT_COUNTEREXAMPLES.md) and [revised decisions](../../../../docs/audit/2026-10-08/full-game/GPT_COMBAT_COUNTEREXAMPLE_REVIEW.md) preserve the conference.\n\n| Topic | Source / conflict | Candidate decision |\n|---|---|---|\n| Initiative, action list, encounter clock | Core 373–418 | Core controls; compatible V6 8066–8244 supplies detailed procedures. |\n| Reaction | Core/V6 summarize one per round; V6 and class integration refresh own turn | NEW explicit interpretation: one resource between regular own-turn starts; no global-round reset. |\n| Stances | Core 419–435; extra-turn ambiguity | Keep listed effects; NEW regular-turn selection and finite allowances. |\n| Movement | Core 436–458; V6 mode switching subtracts physical distance | NEW spent-cost ledger prevents terrain/standing refunds; multiple legal Dashes each add Speed. |\n| Maneuvers | Core 460–486 | Preserve sourced effects; NEW declaration, costs and endpoints fill missing execution details. |\n| Grapple/Shove | V6 opposed procedure vs older shared-class generic DC | Ordinary maneuvers use V6 opposed rolls. Printed card saves/DCs retain their scope. |\n| Prone and Invisible | Core/V6 conflicts already reviewed | Current condition reference controls, including Core ranged Prone disadvantage and Invisible position-reveal exception. |\n| Round duration / simultaneous events | Sources do not give complete ordering | NEW preserved virtual markers and explicit start/end sequence below. |\n| Ready | V6 casts and holds spell; generic Action or movement | Preserve that procedure; NEW one pending state and preparation/release details. |\n| Hide/Search | Core lists actions; V6 unseen attacks | NEW passive DC and observer-specific Hidden procedure using Core task degrees. |\n| Diagonals / terrain composition | 5-ft tile convention; missing full composition | NEW equal orthogonal/diagonal metric and additive movement costs. |\n| Periodic and falling clocks | Existing reviewed overlays | Remain authoritative in their stated scope; no extra-turn tick or duplicated fall step. |\n| Equipment, cargo and Sunder | Core delegates actual object/cargo systems | No invented durability, capacity or automatic cargo protection. These dependencies remain open. |\n\n## Encounter and action resources\n\nEstablish terrain, light, hazards, objectives, exits and cargo; determine awareness; roll `d20 + DEX modifier + legal bonuses`. Highest first. Players choose player/player ties; the Director resolves ties involving NPCs. Keep the resulting ordered slots for the encounter. A round represents approximately six seconds. A **regular turn** is a creature's normal initiative slot even if it cannot act. An extra turn is distinct.\n\nA turn normally permits movement, one Action, a Bonus Action when an actual rule grants a use, and one ordinary free object interaction. A second ordinary interaction or an Action-time object activation uses an Action. Brief communication is not an extra Action. Conditions and specific features may restrict these permissions.\n\nThe shared limit is one **extra full Action per creature per numbered round**. Such an Action cannot grant another extra Action, Bonus Action or Reaction. This cap is independent of Reaction timing. Extra turns do not by themselves refresh Reactions, recharge, periodic ticks, recovery ticks or the regular-turn allowances defined here.\n\n**Reaction interpretation.** Begin an encounter with one available Reaction unless surprised or otherwise prohibited. Refresh at each regular own-turn start; never at the numbered round boundary or an extra turn. A late-initiative creature can therefore react before its first turn, refresh, and react again in the same numbered round. A legal trigger, available resource and all feature prerequisites are still required.\n\n**Explicit additional Reactions.** A reviewed card can grant a separate limited use without refreshing the ordinary resource. Warrior Mind Shock supplies its stated once-per-duration non-attack Reaction; spending one resource does not consume/reset the other. Surprise, Incapacitated and Focused still restrict both. It grants no Counterspell knowledge or waived costs. Extra turns refresh neither. Per-dose reapplication/stacking and other specific grants remain part of the class semantic pass.\n\n**Surprise — V6 plus NEW Bonus Action clarification.** On a surprised creature's first regular turn it cannot voluntarily move or take an Action or Bonus Action. It cannot react until that turn ends. Its slot, durations, passive effects, concentration, periodic effects and recharge still occur. Surprise alone is not Incapacitated or Unconscious. Brief communication and an otherwise legal simple free interaction remain possible.\n\n**Entry — NEW.** Standard end-round reinforcements roll/receive initiative and first act next round. An explicit midround entrant acts this round only if its complete ordered slot is still upcoming; entry during that exact slot counts as already passed. It has its initial Reaction unless prohibited. A summon-specific timing rule overrides this generic entry procedure.\n\nAfter the final slot, resolve Core's end-round hazards, reinforcements, morale, objectives and boss phases. Conclude the scene with surrender, capture, pursuit, harvesting access and noise consequences under their own rules; winning initiative does not resolve those automatically.\n\n## Time and event ordering — NEW\n\nFor a duration created in initiative, record the relevant original regular start/end marker. Existing effects keep that **virtual marker**, repeating every six seconds if the creature leaves or changes initiative. Reordering, rerolling, extra turns, death/removal or leaving/reentering initiative never restart an existing duration. New effects use the current schedule. Source death still ends concentration, a source-dependent aura/tether or an effect explicitly ending on death.\n\n- A bare hostile target-bound **N rounds** ends at the Nth future regular target-end marker. An in-progress target turn's current end does not count.\n- A bare self/source buff, ally-targeted buff or source-controlled aura **N rounds** ends at the Nth future source-start marker. Explicit target-turn, pulse or absolute timing overrides this default. Activation during source turn 1 for three rounds ends at source turn 4's start.\n- **Next turn** means the next future regular turn of the named creature, not the current turn's still-upcoming end. An unqualified next turn refers to source/user under the existing shared rule. Explicit “this turn” can include the current turn.\n- An outside-initiative bare N-round effect instead gets an absolute application-time + N×6 seconds deadline. Entering initiative preserves that deadline.\n- Explicit periodic profiles retain their registered clocks and tick counts. Never create a second timer merely because a generic duration rule also exists.\n\nThe [exact GPT review](../../../../docs/audit/2026-10-08/full-game/GPT_COMBAT_EXACT_FINDINGS.md) confirms ally-buff source clocks and excluding the current turn for explicit next-turn endpoints. The helper excludes a specific current-end marker only if that marker would otherwise be selected: application during a turn at time1 with end3 excludes3 and selects9; application at/after3 also selects9, without erroneously skipping again to15.\n\nAt a regular turn's **start**, resolve in this order:\n\n1. Expire start-ending effects and apply immediate state changes from those expirations.\n2. Refresh Reaction; tick regular-start recharge/non-action clocks.\n3. Resolve already-due absolute state recovery if now legal, including a previously blocked Stable recovery deadline.\n4. If still alive, dying and at 0 HP with death saves, make that turn's death save.\n5. Resolve other start effects and periodic events in application order.\n6. Begin voluntary turn decisions.\n\nAt **end**, resolve triggered effects, due damage, saves and maintenance before end-ending effects expire; then the slot finishes. No extra turn creates another regular marker. The caller must not reuse a resolved marker.\n\nFor simultaneous effects, earlier application timestamp precedes later. For simultaneous components of one source, its printed internal order controls; otherwise use stable application sequence, with source/mechanical ID as a final tie-break. Record that sequence when creating the effects. Beneficial/harmful labels do not change priority. Periodic family arbitration happens once per family/marker before its damage, retaining the family registry's consumed loser ticks.\n\nExamples: dying at 0 rolls its death save before ordinary start-turn healing; a fatal death save is not undone by ordinary healing. Stable at 0 skips the save, then start-turn damage can break Stable. A natural 20 can restore 1 HP before a due Bleed drops it to 0 as a new drop event. If Decay expires while a Stable recovery deadline is already due, the old deadline resolves once without rerolling recovery time.\n\n## Standard Actions\n\n| Action | Procedure |\n|---|---|\n| Attack | Make the attacks legally granted by class and weapon. Replacing one attack does not replace the entire Action unless specified. |\n| Cast | Use an Action-time spell or Resonance technique; all known/prepared, cost, component and target requirements apply. |\n| Dash | Add one current Speed to each mode's turn cap; see movement ledger. Speed 0 grants none. A second legally paid Dash adds another Speed. |\n| Disengage | Voluntary movement does not provoke opportunity attacks for the rest of that turn. |\n| Dodge | Until next regular start, attacks from attackers you can see have disadvantage; your DEX saves have advantage. Benefit fails while Incapacitated or Speed 0. |\n| Guard | +2 AC against the first attack against you before next regular start; consumed on that attack hit or miss. Guard state and Interpose permission remain until expiry even after the +2 is spent. |\n| Aim | Action; no prior voluntary movement this turn, and no subsequent voluntary movement that turn. +2 to one ranged/spell attack before turn end. Forced movement does not cancel it. It grants no attack; a legal Bonus Action/extra Action/express attack must supply one. |\n| Help | Advantage on a relevant ally check before next regular start, or assist an attack against a foe within 5 ft: the helped ally's first attack before that deadline has advantage. Assistance must be physically possible. |\n| Hide / Search | Use the observer-specific procedures below. |\n| Ready | Prepare an Action or movement; release uses Reaction as detailed below. |\n| Use Object | Pay for the second ordinary interaction or an object's required Action activation. No extra hands/capacity are granted. |\n| Improvise | Director declares ability, applicable proficiency, DC/opposed method and intended consequence before rolling. Success does not create unannounced conditions/actions. |\n\n**Brace — NEW.** Action, choose one mode until next regular start. **Intercept:** choose a breach/doorway edge or current melee reach; when a hostile creature charges/leaps/breaches into it and enters legal reach, spend Reaction for one legal melee weapon attack after entry and before later movement/actions. **Hold Ground:** spend Reaction on the first applicable discrete push/pull/shove/knockback to reduce displacement 5 ft, minimum 0. It does not reduce damage, prevent independent Prone, stop gravity/fall steps, currents, conveyors, collapsing ground or teleportation, or grant support/hover. One Brace supplies one opportunity, not an extra Reaction.\n\n**Rally — NEW.** Action; choose one ally within 30 ft able to perceive the rally and one active source-qualified Frightened record. CHA check at that record's source DC, otherwise 10; Success/Critical Success ends only that record. A later reviewed morale rule can supply a separate specific interaction. No automatic temporary HP, loyalty, belief or advantage.\n\n**Withdraw.** Action; separate half-current-Speed movement budget, normal terrain costs, no opportunity attacks from that movement and +2 AC only while that movement resolves. Speed 0 grants none. Moving zero grants no lingering +2. Ordinary movement before/after remains separate. The separate budget is NEW interpretation of Core 480.\n\n**Secure Cargo.** Core 482 preserves the wording “up to one Extraction Load class.” The subsequently reviewed [body and carrying guide](BODY_EQUIPMENT_PLAYTEST.md) supplies the provisional interpretation: spend an Action on one legal attach, lift, transfer or protective operation involving one actual cargo unit or physically constituted bundle with an assigned Extraction Class. Load measures burden; Extraction Class measures danger and logistics. Apply actual capacity, custody, hands, harnesses, checks and durability. The Action grants no extra capacity, equipment, automatic success, invulnerability or reduction in Extraction Class. Full equipment and extraction catalogs remain open.\n\n## Ready\n\nSpend an Action to declare a perceivable trigger and one legal Action-time action or movement. It expires next regular own-turn start. **NEW:** only one pending Ready per creature; replacing it loses the prior setup and any paid held-spell costs. No Bonus Action/Reaction ability, long ritual or multi-minute activity can be prepared by generic Ready.\n\nThe trigger happens first, then the readied response immediately follows before later events, unless a specific effect explicitly interrupts. On release spend the normal Reaction and recheck target, range, sight/perception, line of effect, ammunition and other prerequisites. An invalid or declined occurrence spends no release resource; the Ready can wait for another valid occurrence before expiry. This does not undo setup costs. Once released, it is consumed.\n\nFor ordinary nonspell actions, feature/ammunition costs are paid on release unless text charges setup. A readied **Attack Action** gets its legally available ordinary attack count; a feature limited to your own turn does not thereby work off-turn. Core Overwatch is explicitly one ranged attack, not that full Attack Action and not Soldier's distinct engine.\n\nA readied spell is actually **cast at preparation**: choose spell/mode, verify legality, pay RP/material/other costs, provide components, resolve Dissonance/Refrain and cast-trigger effects once. Counterspell/cast-interruption opportunity occurs then, not again at release. Every held spell requires concentration, even ordinarily non-concentration spells; incompatible prior concentration ends. Lost concentration loses the held spell and costs. NEW: select the legal target/area point at release and recheck legality without repaying. At release the hold continues as ordinary spell concentration if required, otherwise ends after resolution. Expired/replaced/countered held spells do not refund paid costs.\n\nReady movement grants a separate current-Speed budget on release, pays terrain costs and can provoke ordinary opportunity attacks. It grants no extra Action, Bonus Action or interaction.\n\nFocused stance's Reaction restriction also blocks an ordinary Ready release; Ready does not create a Counterspell exception. Holding a spell does not waive any current Reaction prohibition.\n\n**Spell-effect duration begins at release.** Its normal duration is anchored when the effect manifests, while Ready expiry and held concentration already run from preparation. A source explicitly tying a particular consequence to casting/preparation retains that consequence's stated clock. No casting cost, Refrain, Dissonance or cast trigger occurs a second time merely from release.\n\n## Stances\n\nChoose one stance during a **regular** own turn before the first event that would use any stance benefit/restriction: attack, spell, relevant check/save or movement. If none occurs, choose before turn end. No retroactive selection. It lasts until next regular start. NEW: extra turns preserve the stance and spent allowances; they cannot choose/replenish it.\n\n| Stance | Effect |\n|---|---|\n| Aggressive | +2 damage once per qualifying weapon attack's damage packet; −2 AC. Not periodic or copied damage. |\n| Guarded | +1 AC, advantage on explicit roll/contest resisting Shove, Speed −5 (minimum 0). Its +1 can coexist with Guard's first-attack +2, giving +3 then +1. This combination is explicitly allowed here. |\n| Mobile | First 5 ft of actual voluntary movement does not provoke; no free movement is granted. Your opportunity attacks reduce each base weapon die one step: d12→d10→d8→d6→d4 (minimum d4). Rider dice unchanged. Die sequence is NEW. |\n| Focused | Choose +1 save DC for one spell OR +1 to one attack roll from one spell. After use, no-Reaction-except-Counterspell restriction remains through expiry. This grants no Counterspell knowledge, free cost or extra Reaction. |\n| Carrying | Choose one actual currently applicable cargo penalty to ignore; choice fixed through expiry. It does not increase capacity or erase all load limits. DEX saves have disadvantage. |\n\n## Maneuvers and ordinary attacks\n\nAll opposed checks use Core total comparison: higher wins, exact tie preserves state. No natural-20 automatic victory or hidden task-degree effect on an opposed tie. A printed save-based class maneuver keeps its own method.\n\n| Maneuver | Execution |\n|---|---|\n| Grapple | Replace one Attack Action attack; reach and target ≤one size larger. STR Athletics vs defender STR Athletics/DEX Acrobatics. Escape Action uses same opposed contest. NEW one free usable grasping hand/appendage per target; release freely on own turn. Ends if required reach breaks or grappler becomes Incapacitated. No concentration required. Dragging halves Speed unless target ≥two sizes smaller. Magical/card exceptions remain source-specific. |\n| Shove | Replace one attack; same reach/size/contest limits; success pushes 5 ft away or knocks Prone. |\n| Trip | Replace one melee attack, Athletics vs Athletics/Acrobatics, success Prone. NEW same one-size-larger cap. |\n| Disarm | Replace one attack, Athletics or weapon check vs Athletics/Acrobatics. Item must be held and reachable. Attacker chooses legal adjacent unoccupied drop space, otherwise target's own space. Explicit non-droppable bonds prevent it; magic/curse alone does not. No severed attunement/ownership/curse. |\n| Feint | Bonus Action Deception vs Insight; success advantage on next melee attack this turn only. |\n| Sunder | Attack an object/shield/armor component/construct joint using its actual durability system rather than creature HP. Unspecified durability remains an equipment dependency. |\n| Break Formation | Special Shove replacing one attack, same reach/size contest with attacker disadvantage. Success pushes 10 ft; snapshot target's adjacent allies before push and remove their Guard Action benefits. Does not remove Guarded stance or target's own Guard. |\n| Overwatch | Ready one ranged attack against movement in a named lane. Reaction and ammunition paid at release. Distinct from Soldier's class engine. |\n\n**Pin and Silence the Caster — NEW tradeoff.** Declare before rolling and take **−2 to that attack roll**. Each creature has one Pin token and one Silence the Caster token between starts of its regular turns. An unused token may be spent on any otherwise legal qualifying attack, including off-turn, extra-turn or readied attacks. A legal declaration followed by an attack roll consumes the token even on a miss; an illegal declaration rejected before rolling consumes nothing. Tokens refresh only at regular own-turn start, never on an extra turn or numbered-round boundary. One attack cannot combine another optional tactical maneuver. These provisional numbers require table playtesting.\n\nPin uses a ranged weapon attack. On hit deal ordinary damage and apply Speed −10 (floor 0), DC `8 + PB + attack ability modifier`. Ordinary Pins do not stack the penalty. At target's regular start, one optional STR Athletics task check against the highest active ordinary Pin DC ends all ordinary Pins on Success/Critical Success. Alternatively one Action removes all automatically. Source-specific card Pins do not silently acquire this generic definition or removal scope.\n\nSilence the Caster uses a melee attack. On hit prevent verbal-component Reactions until target's next regular start. If concentrating, make exactly one CON concentration save at `max(10, floor(post-mitigation damage/2))`, including DC10 when damage is zero. This fulfills, rather than duplicates, the normal damage concentration check. It is not Core Silenced: speech and Action-time verbal casting remain possible.\n\n**Interpose.** While Guarding, after an adjacent ally becomes an attack target but before its roll, spend Reaction and become target if legal for that attack (range/type/line/cover). NEW may move **up to** 5 ft using a separate terrain-paying budget, including zero if already legally positioned. Speed0 permits the zero-movement case only. It redirects one attack without cancelling or duplicating it. Guard's unspent +2 applies if this is the first attack against you. Voluntary movement has ordinary opportunity-attack consequences.\n\n**Press.** On a melee hit during your regular turn, arm one target until next regular start. While unconsumed, another qualifying hit during that turn may replace it. If the target voluntarily retreats from an adjacent space, follow up to 5 ft into the vacated space using a separate terrain-paying budget; no Action/BA/Reaction. It can provoke ordinary opportunity attacks. No following teleport/forced movement. Once used, cannot rearm before next regular turn, including during an extra turn. Speed0 prevents movement.\n\n**Opportunity attack.** Spend Reaction for one melee attack against a seen hostile creature voluntarily leaving reach, immediately **before** exit. Forced movement/teleport do not trigger; Disengage suppresses it for its stated turn. Specific reviewed triggers may override these conditions.\n\n**Two-weapon fighting.** After taking Attack and attacking with a Light melee weapon in one hand, spend Bonus Action for one attack with a different Light melee weapon in the other hand. Do not add a positive ability modifier to that attack's damage; include a negative modifier. This grants neither another Bonus Action nor a class feature.\n\n## Movement and geometry\n\nTrack **spent movement cost**, not distance, for ordinary turn movement. Mode cap = `(1 + number of legal Dash actions this turn) × current Speed for that mode`; remaining = max(0, cap − spent cost). Switching modes never refunds cost. Recalculate cap on Speed changes, preserving spent cost. Stand from Prone costs floor(half current Speed) and requires nonzero Speed and enough budget. Dropping Prone costs no movement.\n\nFor each foot traveled, normal/difficult/severe terrain costs 1/2/3; use strongest terrain only. Add +1 for crawling and +1 for squeezing. Crawl in difficult =3:1; crawl+squeeze in difficult =4:1. A nonhostile occupied space counts as difficult terrain. Enter a hostile creature's space only with ≥two-size difference or an express feature; never willingly end in an occupied space. Squeeze through a space one size smaller, with own attack/DEX-save disadvantage and attacks-against advantage.\n\nWalk10 through difficult spends20; switch to Fly30 leaves10. Stand with Speed30 spends15; switching to Fly30 leaves15. Two legal Dashes at Speed30 give cap90; prior expenditure still counts. Interpose/Press grant5 budget: they cannot enter a difficult 5-ft tile costing10. Their separate budgets never refill ordinary movement. Ready movement uses Speed at release; Withdraw uses half current Speed. All voluntary movement requires nonzero relevant Speed.\n\nForced movement does not use these ledgers or provoke ordinary opportunity attacks, cannot place creatures in solid matter, and stops at obstacles. Teleport does not traverse intervening terrain or spend Speed unless printed; it requires a legal destination. Speed0 alone does not block forced movement or teleport. Flight with Prone/Speed0/lost support causes Falling unless Hover or an explicit magical support exception applies; use the existing fixed Fall Marker procedure. Reaching support ends Falling.\n\nNEW grid metric: each orthogonal or diagonal neighboring tile is 5 ft; use the same metric for ordinary ranges/areas. No diagonal cut through a corner when both adjacent orthogonal sides are completely blocked. Exact templates, 3D geometry, mounts, chases, jumping, climbing/swimming procedures and currents need further source review.\n\n## Cover, sight, Hide and Search\n\nKeep **visibility**, **known location**, and **line of effect** separate. Seeing through a barrier does not itself permit attacking through it. Best cover only: half +2 AC/DEX saves; three-quarters +5; total prevents direct targeting through that obstruction. Never add +2 and +5. For areas, evaluate line/cover from the area's origin to each target; total cover there excludes a creature unless the effect explicitly travels around/through the obstruction. The origin point itself must be legally targetable.\n\nWithin normal ranged range, no range penalty; beyond normal through long, disadvantage; beyond long, illegal. Ranged attacks also have disadvantage when within5ft of a non-Incapacitated hostile that can see the attacker, even if the attacker cannot see that hostile. All advantage/disadvantage follows the single cancellation rule.\n\nNEW **Hide**: permitted only against observers unable to see you clearly or where a feature permits. Roll DEX Stealth once, record natural die and total. Against each observer's passive DC `10 + WIS Perception modifier including applicable proficiency`, use the Core four-degree task system. Meet DC succeeds before natural1/20 degree adjustment; Success/Critical Success establishes Hidden for that observer. No unsourced passive ±5 adjustment for advantage/disadvantage. Actual Search rolls use their normal modifiers.\n\nHidden means that observer cannot see you and does not know your location. Clear sight or learning your position ends Hidden for that observer. Moving between hiding places can retain Hidden only if no clear sight/revealing event occurs. A failed Hide cannot simply be rerolled unchanged; a materially new hiding position, concealment, distraction, sight-breaking event or express reroll feature is required. Hidden is not a generic immunity or a free stealth roll for every move.\n\n**Search** Action: WIS Perception for sensory detection; INT Investigation for appropriate traces/deduction; Core Arcana/Engineering where relevant. Directly locating a Hidden creature defaults to Perception against its recorded final Stealth total as a static DC, using task degrees/natural shifts. Success reveals location only to the searcher and ends Hidden for that observer, without automatically granting sight. Communicate normally to inform others. Search can be repeated on later turns by paying the Action.\n\nAttacks against unseen targets have disadvantage; unseen attackers have advantage, subject to the **Core Invisible exception**: once its position is known, Invisible's own attack modifiers end for that observer and cannot immediately return solely because it remains Invisible. Independent darkness, smoke, blindness or hiding can still supply modifiers.\n\nResolve an attack using pre-attack information; afterward reveal location to observers able to perceive its evidence. If an unseen target's location is unknown, do not directly choose it for a creature-target effect. A weapon/projectile can instead attack a suspected space: wrong space automatically misses; correct space uses unseen rules. A “creature you can see” requirement is never met by position knowledge alone. Area effects can target legal points under their own rules.\n\n## Scope and validation\n\n[Reference checks](COMBAT_PLAYTEST_VALIDATION.json) exercise selected budgets, clocks and edge cases. They do not implement map visibility, inventory, feature legality, complete event dispatch, all maneuvers or a live application. No claim of encounter balance or human playtest completion follows from a passing report.\n\n`start_life_sequence` receives already-resolved/arbitrated HP events; it does not perform condition-family arbitration, cure processing, trigger generation, duration expiry, target legality, defense calculations or general simultaneous-event dispatch. `hide_results` answers only whether Hidden is established, collapsing the two success degrees and two failure degrees; it does not retain the full task-degree record. The Reaction helper models the ordinary resource, not additional Reaction grants printed on specific cards.\n\nRemaining work includes cargo terminology/capacity/durability; detailed movement/environment/chases; area templates and sensory abilities; summons/boss full-turn examples; all-source class/spell semantic interactions and specific resource grants; and the whole-game ledger's other acceptance gates.\n"},{"file":"DescentReviewChanges/ReviewBible/docs/mechanics/CHARACTER_CREATION_PLAYTEST.md","sha256":"e59456f4430f7f72db0392c3a52be8ec8b7d804b4b765c0a73f81c72f060a49e","heading":"entire guide","exactText":"# Create and advance a character\n\n**REVIEWED CHARACTER FOUNDATION — authored operational PLAYTEST candidate.** The [source conference](../../../../docs/audit/2026-10-08/full-game/GPT_CHARACTER_FOUNDATION_CONFERENCE.md) and [exact review](../../../../docs/audit/2026-10-08/full-game/GPT_CHARACTER_FOUNDATION_EXACT_REVIEW.md), with both requested wording repairs applied, settle the class foundations and advancement rules below. Use the [17-class tables](CHARACTER_FOUNDATION_TABLES.md) beside this guide. Full ancestry traits, background packages and equipment records still need their own reconciliation; this foundation does not certify a complete ready-to-play character or live companion parity.\n\n## Begin a character sheet\n\n1. **Record the concept and first Base class.** Choose one of the 17 classes. Start at character level 1 and that class’s level 1. Keep a chronological level record and the order in which you enter classes. Arch and Hero are later stages within a class, not additional Base classes. The former Fighter/Swashbuckler entries and separate historical prestige records supply no extra starting class or automatic grants.\n2. **Generate six abilities.** Choose the method before generating scores: roll four d6, discard the lowest and total the other three, six times; or assign the standard array 15, 14, 13, 12, 10, 8. Assign those six results once to STR, DEX, CON, INT, WIS and CHA. Keep the original rolls. Do not reroll, mix methods or select a preferred set without a separate table rule. The optional provisional point-buy method below is also available.\n3. **Record ancestry and any required variant.** Apply its actual ability increases once after assigning scores, resolving an OR choice once. Record size, movement, senses, languages and each complete trait procedure from its source. Trait names alone are insufficient. Do not stack alternative builder/racial-variant entries onto the same book trait. Full mechanical reconciliation of the 14 ancestries is still pending. This step grants no authority to revise their cosmology or origin lore.\n4. **Choose one background.** Record its actual skills, tools, languages, feature and starting package, including required choices. Complete background procedures remain a separate source pass. The source permits a custom background with two skills, two tools/languages, equipment and a Reputation-related feature developed with the Director; that permission does not supply an invented unlimited feature or a second background.\n5. **Apply starting training.** Use this first class’s saves, armor, weapons, tools and number of skill choices from the foundation tables. Use the class list for each skill choice. Add ancestry/background training with the duplicate procedure below. Proficiency grants training, not equipment ownership, spells or a second PB on one roll.\n6. **Set HP, Hit Dice and proficiency.** First-character-level base HP is the maximum face of this class’s HD; add current CON modifier, minimum 1. Record one unspent Hit Die of that size. At level 1 PB is +2. Keep base maximum HP separate from later effects, reductions and systemic multipliers.\n7. **Acquire the actual class kit.** Follow the class’s own level-1 engine, automatic grants and scheduled choices. Ordinary classes start with four Base choices, at least two active, where their schedule says so. Druid, Oracle and Paladin have explicit schedules; follow those instead. Make required engine/path choices, such as Oracle Mystery and Curse, exactly when their cards require them. Use the [class library](CLASS_PLAYTEST_INDEX.md), [legal acquisition examples](CLASS_PLAYTEST_BUILD_EXAMPLES.md), and [Paladin oath pairs](PALADIN_OATHS_AND_OATHBREAKERS.md). No legacy Skill Points, companion feature strings or historical prestige rows add grants to this schedule.\n8. **Record magic and class resources.** Use the class’s current Core V2 RP table and rank gates, plus its actual known/prepared/spellbook procedure. Record each selected operational spell’s identity and casting class. Enough RP never unlocks a rank or school. Oracle uses Revelation Charges; Soldier and Warrior have no general RP casting. Initial class resources and empty stores follow their actual engine setup; preparation or copying is not waived. Read [shared class rules](CLASS_PLAYTEST_RULES.md) and [spell integration](SPELL_INTEGRATION_RULES.md).\n9. **Choose starting equipment or money.** Take the class and background packages, **or** roll the first class’s starting wealth and buy equipment with it. Wealth replaces all starting package equipment, including package money; background training and features remain. Record purchases, remaining coins, ammunition, components, containers, quantities and actual weight/Load. An item must exist with usable statistics; a proficiency or feature label cannot create it. The equipment pass must still settle missing profiles, prices and eligibility tags.\n10. **Finish the derived sheet.** Calculate ability modifiers, trained saves and skills, legal equipped AC, movement, weapon attacks, class attacks/DCs and actual resource caps. Choose one applicable AC formula rather than adding formulas. A normal proficient attack is d20 + PB + its actual attack modifier; class DC is 8 + PB + its actual class modifier. Use actual negative modifiers; the class card’s minimum-1 effect modifier is not a minimum attack/DC modifier. Note conditions, Wounds, supplies and other scene state separately. Record the source/card for every nonordinary number.\n\nAn ancestry/background feature, spell or item that remains mechanically unresolved is a visible unfinished dependency. A checked box or a saved companion character does not resolve that dependency.\n\n## Abilities and training\n\nAbility modifier = floor((score − 10) / 2), including negative results. For example, 8 gives −1, 13 gives +1 and 16 gives +3.\n\n**Optional new candidate method:** 27-point buy, scores 8–15 before ancestry. Costs are 8:0, 9:1, 10:2, 11:3, 12:4, 13:5, 14:7, 15:9. Spend no more than 27 points across six scores; no score outside that range. This adopts the existing companion method as a provisional option, not a claim that the book already specified it.\n\nResolve duplicate training as follows:\n\n- For a selectable grant, choose another eligible option from that grant’s own list.\n- For a fixed ancestry/background skill grant with no alternative list, choose another of the 20 basic skills.\n- For a duplicated tool, choose another eligible tool from that grant’s list; if it has no list, use another actually supported tool in the same broad category.\n- Duplicate armor, weapon, save or multiclass chassis training gives no skill or feat compensation. A new multiclass skill selection must still use its own permitted list.\n- Duplicate proficiency never creates Expertise unless an exact feature says so. If a proficiency grant requires a choice and every legal option for that grant is already possessed, that grant provides no additional proficiency. Character creation, multiclass entry or advancement remains legal. Do not substitute a feat, Expertise, another category of proficiency, an unrelated skill/tool, or a banked future selection. This applies to skill and tool lists, including a legal replacement list or category that is itself exhausted.\n\nPB is applied once to a check even if a skill and tool both qualify. An exact Expertise multiplier modifies that one proficiency contribution; it does not add another independent PB.\n\n## Gain a level\n\nOpening campaigns officially use milestones. The Director’s announced milestones grant advancement; a CR-to-XP UI helper is not the official Opening reward law. The book’s cumulative XP thresholds remain available for tables using optional XP. See [XP provenance](XP_ADVANCEMENT_CANON.md).\n\nEach earned advancement adds one total character level, to a combined maximum of 20. Choose one existing Base class to advance, or enter one new Base class if eligible. Process multiple earned levels one at a time; do not skip intervening choices.\n\n1. Record the gained class and new class level. Recalculate total level and PB: +2 at total 1–4, +3 at 5–8, +4 at 9–12, +5 at 13–16, +6 at 17–20.\n2. Add one new unspent Hit Die of the gained class’s size. Previously spent Hit Dice remain spent; gaining this die does not refresh any earlier die. For HP, roll that die or use its fixed rounded-up average: d6→4, d8→5, d10→6, d12→7. Record the base result before CON. This includes taking a new class’s first level after character level 1; maximum first-level HP is never granted again.\n3. Apply exactly the gained class’s acquisition row, automatic improvements and class-level prerequisites. Complete its choices and any newly reached Arch/Hero gate. Automatic grants, scheduled selections and ASIs are distinct; do not pay a selection twice or grant an extra selection because the same feature appears in a legacy table.\n4. At an ASI milestone, increase one score by 2 or two scores by 1, normally no higher than 20, or select one eligible operational feat. Ordinary milestones are class levels 4/8/12/16/19; Oracle uses 4/8/14/19. Follow any already-reviewed specific exception. No starting feat or repeatable feat is implied. [Eight complete feat options](FEAT_PLAYTEST_INDEX.md) do not make the other 327 complete.\n5. Update legal repertoire, prepared capacity, class scaling and resource maxima at the gained **class** level. Use class-specific Druid preparation, Wizard spellbook/matrices and other explicit rules before generic acquisition defaults. New capacity does not provide an unnamed spell, an unearned choice, a filled Echo record or an extra prepared spell without the required preparation procedure.\n6. Recompute base maximum HP from all attained-level records using current CON: sum(max(1, each stored base value + current CON modifier)). Then apply the separate [maximum-HP ledger](MAXIMUM_HP_PLAYTEST.md), including compatible bonuses, reductions and systemic factors. Do not feed an already modified maximum back in as the base.\n7. Keep current HP and spent resources unchanged except for a downward clamp when a maximum falls. An ordinary level-up is not healing or a rest: it cannot revive a downed/dead character, erase Wounds or refill already spent Hit Dice, RP, RC, class pools, item charges or uses. Newly granted features still follow an explicit acquisition/initialization clause if they have one; “gain a maximum” alone does not mean “restore current.”\n\n**New HP completion:** each level’s HP contribution has a minimum of 1. Recompute those individual floors after permanent CON changes. This preserves retroactive CON adjustment without a naive level×modifier-change formula that can bypass a floor. Capacity increases grant no current HP; decreases can clamp current HP without damage/healing triggers, consistent with the reviewed maximum-HP interface. Temporary ability effects follow their own actual source rules rather than being silently treated as permanent advancement.\n\nNo banked card selections, free retraining or free respec is introduced. Use an existing explicit replacement/retraining procedure where one exists.\n\n## Multiclass entry and class features\n\nWhen entering a new Base class, meet the multiclass score prerequisites for **every held class and the new class**. The foundation tables give the exact source gates; a class’s descriptive primary ability is not a substitute. These requirements are checked on new-class entry. Losing a qualifying score later does not erase earned classes/features, though it can prevent entering another new class. Advancing a class already held is not a new-class entry test.\n\nOnly the first character class grants its full starting saves and proficiency package. Each later newly entered class grants its separate multiclass proficiency row once. It grants no second background, starting wealth/equipment or full starting skill count. Its genuine level-1 engine/features and acquisition schedule still apply.\n\n**New chassis clarification:** a level-1 card whose sole function is to restate starting armor/weapon proficiency cannot bypass the multiclass table. Paladin Martial Proficiency therefore supplies no heavy-armor bypass. An independent non-chassis mechanic in a feature remains available on its actual terms.\n\nUse the largest equivalent Extra Attack count, never their sum. Use one applicable AC formula, never their sum. Duplicate historical Channel Divinity labels do not manufacture another use pool. Distinct operational class engines and paid techniques remain distinct and obey their own costs and the shared action limits.\n\nClass level controls rank access, acquisitions, S scaling, Arch/Hero gates and finales. Total character level controls PB and optional XP thresholds. A Bard 5 / Mage 5 is total 10 with PB +4, but neither class has reached its Hero gate.\n\n## One shared RP pool\n\nCalculate each eligible class’s own current candidate RP pool at its own class level. Sort these classes by:\n\n1. Caster tier: Full (Cleric, Druid, Mage, Wizard), then Half (Paladin, Ranger), then Hybrid (Bard, Shaman, Mirrorsage, Crimson Agent), then Minor (Dragoon, Monk, Thief, Tinker).\n2. Within a tier, higher class level first.\n3. If class levels tie, higher actual RP pool first.\n4. If both tie, the class acquired earlier first. Record that order; do not choose a different primary per cast.\n\nOracle, Soldier and Warrior contribute no RP pool. Maximum RP = the first class’s full pool + ceil(one quarter of the second class’s pool). With only one eligible class use its pool; with none, maximum RP is 0. Third and later eligible classes contribute no extra capacity, while retaining their earned class features and legal repertoire. Use current Core V2 candidate pools, not the older smaller companion-derived tables.\n\nAll RP-paid spells and techniques use this one merged pool unless an exact rule names another resource. RC, BG, Momentum, Ki, Conviction, Lay on Hands and other class resources remain separate. A change in the primary class or maximum is not recovery: preserve current RP and clamp only if above the new maximum. This can reduce maximum RP when a newly entered higher-tier class becomes primary; do not silently choose a larger lower-tier pool instead.\n\nEach ordinary cast declares its casting class before costs. That class must independently know/prepare the spell, meet its school/rank gate and supply its own casting ability. Another class’s levels or repertoire cannot fill those requirements. Where two classes know the same spell, choosing its casting class does not create a second spell identity.\n\n## Shared strain, recovery and concentration\n\nKeep one character-wide Dissonance number and one Refrain history across classes. For a cast, evaluate limit = max(4, 2 + total-character PB + max(0, the actual casting-class modifier)). Apply the existing surcharges, ranked-cast cutoff, repetition and explicit class exceptions from [shared class rules](CLASS_PLAYTEST_RULES.md). Switching casting class does not clear either history; the same spell identity still repeats. Class cadence benefits require their actual ownership and trigger conditions. Idle decay and Grounding occur once per character turn, not once per class.\n\nGeneric Retune happens once per eligible completed short rest for the character. It restores max(0, round-half-up(maximum RP / 4) + the current primary RP class’s casting modifier), capped at the merged maximum, and clears Dissonance. Optional once/day meditation uses that same primary class’s positive modifier and the existing PB-based strain reduction. Long-rest RP recovery restores the merged pool under the existing recovery rules. None of these generic procedures duplicates because the character has more classes or recovers a different named resource without its own rule.\n\nOracle Revelations use RC and do not generate ordinary Weave strain unless expressly stated; they do not clear existing strain/Refrain from another class. One ordinary concentration slot is shared across classes, with only the exact already-reviewed linked-effect exceptions such as Spell Tether, Tethered Cast and Crimson Harmony. No generic Overcast option is reintroduced.\n\n## Worked foundation checks\n\n- **First Bard level:** CON 14 gives modifier +2; base HP 8+2 = 10, one d8 Hit Die and PB +2. Choose three class skills from the 20-skill list and four eligible Base cards with at least two active, as the Bard schedule directs. Background/ancestry grants remain separate and must be fully specified before play.\n- **Bard 1→2 without a rest:** fixed HP base 5 and CON +2 increase base maximum 10→17. If current HP was 6 it stays 6. The second Bard level grants its actual two Base selections. A higher RP maximum does not refill spent RP.\n- **New class does not repeat maximum HP:** a Bard 3 taking Mage 1 adds d6 roll or fixed 4, plus CON; it does not add 6 merely because Mage is class level 1. Total PB is still +2 at character level 4. The character must meet Bard CHA 13 and Mage INT 13 before entry.\n- **Retroactive CON with floors:** a d6 first level recorded as 6 and a later d6 roll recorded as 1 give contributions 4 and 1 at CON 6 (−2), total 5. At CON 8 (−1) they give 5 and 1, total 6. Adding a naive two levels × +1 would incorrectly give 7.\n- **A three-class caster:** Cleric 6, Druid 6 and Wizard 1 use Cleric/Druid as the two Full-tier contributors because class level precedes pool size. Their own level-6 pools are 15 each, giving 15+ceil(15/4) = 19 RP; earlier acquisition breaks their tie. Wizard 1 adds no RP but retains its own legal Wizard 1 spell access. Total 13 supplies PB +5; no class has a Hero gate.\n- **Different casting stats:** at total level 6, PB +3, a modifier +3 caster has limit 8 and a modifier +1 caster has limit 6. Shared Dissonance 6 is below the first limit but at the second. Casting through the second class uses its surcharge; the shared strain number and spell repetition history survive the switch.\n- **Monk wealth:** rolling 5d4 for a total of 12 gives 12 gp, not 120 gp. No background-package money is added if this money method replaces the packages.\n- **Later Paladin:** a non-Paladin’s entry uses the Paladin multiclass row. Its level-1 Martial Proficiency restatement grants no extra heavy armor or second set of saving-throw proficiencies.\n\nThe [reference checks](CHARACTER_FOUNDATION_VALIDATION.json) verify selected calculations and source preservation. They do not execute a live character builder, certify every acquisition interaction or replace human playtesting.\n\n## Source decisions and remaining work\n\nThe foundation uses reviewed active class contracts first, then detailed class entries, the Classes Table as fallback and companion data only for actual gaps. The [source evidence](../../../../docs/audit/2026-10-08/full-game/CHARACTER_FOUNDATION_SOURCE_EVIDENCE.json) retains every disputed chassis entry, original skill list and source hash. All original books, legacy UI data and class cards remain unchanged.\n\nNew candidate decisions are explicitly identified: missing class skill lists, Oracle’s starting Arcana substitution, Tinker’s Engineering option, bounded favored/blade weapon choices, optional point buy, duplicate-grant resolution, per-level HP floors and capacity-only advancement, precise multiclass entry/chassis rules, final RP tie-breaking and shared strain/recovery interpretation. These are authored playtest rules rather than approved-book rewrites.\n\nNext dependencies are the complete 14-ancestry and 20-background mechanical pass, equipment and feat acquisition, remaining spells, the expedition-to-Haven loop and final joint flow review. Broader runtime, balance and publication remain separate. Cosmology stays untouched until after the authorized GitHub push has succeeded and its remote commit is verified.\n"},{"file":"DescentReviewChanges/ReviewBible/docs/mechanics/BODY_EQUIPMENT_PLAYTEST.md","sha256":"d93f9cb0ec8a8398560511f23029d38d9b9a8b901e816373579c8f72b0bec1f3","heading":"entire guide","exactText":"# Bodies equipment and carrying\n\n**REVIEWED — authored tabletop body, equipment, carrying and continuous meld procedures.** New integration mechanics remain explicitly provisional; runtime implementation is pending. These procedures connect the [Druid form rules](FORM_TEMP_HP_PLAYTEST.md) and [transformation spells](TRANSFORMATION_SPELL_PLAYTEST.md). They apply to the same character, HP record and physical belongings throughout a change. They grant no additional forms, spell access, actions or resources. The approved books remain preserved.\n\n## Entering and changing forms\n\nBefore paying for a voluntary change, choose a legal form and size, check its space at your current position, and identify any equipment that must be dropped. If the destination or required drops are illegal, the change does not begin and spends nothing. Otherwise pay the source's normal costs and resolve its actual entry effects once. A successful hostile or forced transformation cannot be prevented merely by excess gear: resolve it under its source rules, then reconcile excess equipment by keeping it external, dropping it or recording pending placement. Other source requirements still apply.\n\nYour Druid form uses your own HP, ability scores, saves, proficiency and conditions. It supplies only its printed anatomy and features. Speech does not imply hands: none of the 26 current Druid profiles grants ordinary hand-based equipment use. Of the 12 spell profiles, only Humanoid grants hands. Each profile retains its exact size and movement permissions.\n\n| Change | Equipment procedure |\n|---|---|\n| Root body to first Druid form | Record and meld the equipment physically worn, held or carried by your body, including carried containers and their contents. |\n| Druid form to another Druid form | Keep the same original melded equipment. End the former form's dependent effects and start the new form under its normal rules. Reconcile newly acquired external items with the new anatomy. |\n| A successful spell replaces a Druid or spell form | Close the former transformation and capture the legitimate resulting gear under the new spell's meld rules in one continuous change. There is no moment when you can use the returning equipment. |\n| Shapechange changes its current spell profile | Keep compatible external items. Drop incompatible external items; they do not join the original melded equipment. |\n| Suppression | Follow the spell's suppression procedure. It is not an ending or a fresh capture of equipment. Original melded gear stays inaccessible. |\n| Final reversion, expiry or forced ending | Return to root anatomy, end the source's dependent effects and reconcile surviving equipment as described below. |\n\nThe continuing meld record across Druid-to-Druid changes is a **new provisional integration rule**. It prevents shifting from opening an equipment-use window between forms. A spell replacement retains the already-reviewed close-and-capture procedure.\n\n## What melds\n\nRecord where each item was before the first change: worn in a particular slot, held in a particular hand, or carried in a particular container or on the body. Ownership alone is insufficient. A sword stored in a wagon stays in the wagon when its owner becomes a Hawk. A legitimately borrowed sword in your actual custody can meld and return normally; the lender remains its owner. Changing ownership metadata does not itself move an inaccessible physical item.\n\nA backpack and each of its contents remain distinct items. Count each item's own weight or Load once; a container does not erase its contents. Melded items remain in your custody but are inaccessible. You cannot withdraw or add contents, use components, consume supplies, fire ammunition or activate equipment through the meld.\n\nMelding never repairs damage, reloads a weapon, restores charges, replenishes supplies or creates a replacement item. Destruction, consumption and legitimate transfers remain real. Keep the current condition and quantity of each actual item.\n\n## Using equipment in a form\n\n| Item or effect | Rule |\n|---|---|\n| Original armor and shield | Melded and unusable. Exclude their worn or held AC benefits from the form's normal AC. |\n| Held weapon, tool, focus or material component | Requires actual access and the appropriate anatomy. A form without hands receives no general permission to use it. |\n| Effect requiring an item to be worn, held or equipped | Suspended while the item is melded. |\n| Effect requiring only possession or attunement | May continue only when its exact rule is independent of the lost anatomy and physical use. An unspecified magic bonus is not automatically operative. |\n| Newly acquired armor, harness, collar or container | Must fit the actual size and anatomy and have a legal way to remain attached or carried. Hands are required for held items, not automatically for every wearable item. |\n| Newly acquired external gear when the form changes | Remains external if compatible. Otherwise use the normal drop procedure. It does not silently join an existing meld record. |\n\nThe possession-versus-use distinction and individual compatibility checks are **new provisional defaults**. A specific item or form can supply an explicit exception. Do not infer one from a creature's name, appearance or ability to speak. Item-specific compatibility across the full equipment catalog remains to be authored.\n\n## Returning equipment\n\nResolve a final reversion in this order:\n\n1. Keep current external items that still exist and remain legally equipped or carried by the root body.\n2. Return each surviving melded item that remains in the character's legitimate custody to its recorded former slot or container, if available and legal.\n3. If an external item now occupies that slot, leave it there. Return the former item as carried inventory if there is a legal place and sufficient capacity.\n4. Drop an item that cannot be carried or equipped at the current position or the nearest legal adjacent item position.\n5. If no legal drop position exists, record that item's placement as pending. It continues to exist; it is not duplicated, destroyed or used to displace another creature.\n\nCurrent external equipment taking priority over a former slot is a **new provisional integration rule**. A returned sword may go into available carried storage instead of replacing a sword you actually acquired and equipped during the transformation. Both retain their current condition and ownership.\n\nDestroyed, consumed, physically transferred, lost or otherwise unavailable items never reappear. An item in another custodian's possession cannot return merely because you still claim ownership of it. Someone else's legal ownership does not prevent an intact borrowed item from returning to your continuing legitimate custody.\n\n## Placement and involuntary endings\n\nA transformation does not grant movement. A voluntary size change keeps the same spatial anchor and must fit before payment. Preserve the existing footprint on a same-size change when possible.\n\nA mandatory ending still occurs if the root anatomy does not fit. First try its normal space at the same position, then a legal squeeze under the [movement rules](COMBAT_ACTION_PLAYTEST.md). If neither fits, record a placement conflict. The **new provisional placement procedure** permits only a nearest legal adjacent placement in the same unobstructed region. It cannot pass through a wall, closed barrier or occupied space. Choose the minimum displacement that fits the whole body; among ties, preserve the greatest overlap with its previous footprint. If still tied, the Director uses a fixed neutral board order, not a tactical choice by the affected player. If no adjacent candidate fits, the conflict remains until the obstruction is removed or a legal relocation effect resolves it. Do not search farther afield.\n\nThis mandatory normalization spends no movement and triggers no opportunity attack, charge distance, movement benefit or other movement rider. It changes no remaining movement allowance and grants no invented crushing damage. It does not permit a voluntary form change to bypass its preflight.\n\nTrack actual height and terrain. Losing flight while unsupported starts ordinary falling from the current location; reversion never moves the creature directly to the ground. Ending a burrowing form underground does not move the creature to the surface. Master of Vermin's permission to pass through Tiny gaps changes clearance, not its printed size.\n\nUse the actual space of the expressed anatomy:\n\n| Size | Space | On a grid of 2.5-foot subcells |\n|---|---|---|\n| Tiny | 2.5 by 2.5 feet | 1 subcell |\n| Small | 5 by 5 feet | 2 by 2 subcells |\n| Medium | 5 by 5 feet | 2 by 2 subcells |\n| Large | 10 by 10 feet | 4 by 4 subcells |\n\nV6 explicitly supplies Tiny and Medium space. Small and Large complete this bounded table provisionally; the supplied V6 passage does not separately list them. Size category still matters even when two sizes occupy the same space. Squeezing changes clearance and its penalties; it does not change size, carrying capacity or weight. Exact Tiny occupancy requires subcells or equivalent measurement, not treating Tiny as Medium.\n\n## Body weight and conditions\n\n**New provisional rule:** a creature-form transformation preserves the body's existing base physical weight unless an exact rule changes it. Size alone does not change weight. A 180-pound character therefore remains 180 pounds as a Tiny Hawk or Large Bear. This is a magical transformation rule, not a claim about the weight of ordinary animals. It applies equally to the current 26 Druid and 12 creature-form spell profiles.\n\nRecord base body weight separately from equipment and condition effects. Petrified multiplies current body weight by ten once; it does not rewrite the base weight or multiply repeatedly for multiple applications. Ending or changing a form does not cure that condition. Temporary Druid form ends under its existing Incapacitated rule, then Petrified continues to apply to the reverted body. An anatomy permitted to persist keeps its own base weight and the same condition overlay.\n\nTrue Polymorph object anatomy uses its actual documented output weight. Its output ceiling remains the lower of current effective input weight and earliest recorded origin weight. Include a current applicable weight-changing condition in the input, but never rebase the origin ceiling upward. Equipment, casualty Load and estimated pounds do not establish material available for a transformation. Creature-origin object anatomy has no independent sale, salvage or harvest value.\n\nFor another creature, mount, floor or platform supporting you, count your effective body weight plus all actual equipment in your custody, including melded items, once. For your own inventory capacity, count what you carry, not your own body weight. Conditions change only the items or body their exact rules affect.\n\n## Carrying and Load\n\nUse pounds for actual weight and Load for inventory burden. Extraction Class describes cargo danger and attention. A casualty's Load describes the difficulty of transporting a body; it is not the body's weight. These quantities answer different questions and never convert automatically into each other.\n\nThe following is a **new provisional reconciliation** of V6 physical handling with the staged Gameplay Loop inventory rules. It does not promote the historical Gameplay Loop file into the approved books. V6's optional encumbrance variant remains preserved as an alternative; do not add its penalties to this Load procedure.\n\n| Carrier size | Physical carry limit in pounds | Push, drag or lift limit in pounds | Inventory capacity in Load | Casualty Load for a body of this size |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| Tiny | Strength score × 7.5 | Strength score × 15 | Strength score ÷ 2 | 1.5 |\n| Small | Strength score × 15 | Strength score × 30 | Maximum of 0 and Strength score − 2 | 3 |\n| Medium | Strength score × 15 | Strength score × 30 | Strength score | 6 |\n| Large | Strength score × 30 | Strength score × 60 | Strength score × 2 | 12 |\n\nUse the character's current Strength score, not the modifier or an imported monster score. Tiny's Inventory capacity and casualty Load are new provisional additions. The pound limits come from V6; Small, Medium and Large Load values come from the Gameplay Loop. Larger unsupported transport sizes require their own completed rule before this table can handle them.\n\nKeep fractional Loads without rounding. An item with a printed Load uses that value. If it has only a known weight, its **provisional Load is pounds divided by ten**. A Load-only item may use ten pounds per Load as a clearly labeled estimate for ordinary hauling; this is insufficient for effects requiring documented physical mass.\n\nCount a container's own empty Load plus the contents' Loads, unless an explicitly inclusive Load already covers those exact contents. Handle pounds separately: sum the empty container weight and contents' weights, or use an explicitly inclusive gross weight for the contents it covers. An inclusive Load does not make a printed weight inclusive, or vice versa. Apply this rule through nested containers and meld budgets so every item counts exactly once in each quantity.\n\nWith positive carried Load at or above half capacity and no greater than capacity, you are **Burdened**: reduce Speed by 10 feet and apply disadvantage to checks to climb, swim or move silently. An empty inventory is not Burdened, including at zero capacity. Above capacity, that inventory cannot be carried routinely; drop or transfer something, or use a legal alternative such as dragging or a vehicle. These threshold and zero-Load details are provisional clarifications.\n\nApply actual physical limits when handling a known weight. Between physical carry capacity and twice that capacity, pushing or dragging uses the lower of otherwise-current Speed and 5 feet. A Speed of 0 stays 0; a Speed of 3 stays 3. Lifting does not independently permit movement: moving while supporting a weight must satisfy carrying, pushing, dragging or another exact rule. An anatomy restriction, such as Hawk's prohibition on carrying a Medium ally, still applies even if numbers would fit. Burden penalties do not create hands, harnesses, lift or access to melded gear.\n\nTransporting a creature must pass both checks: the carrier can physically handle the body's effective weight and associated equipment, and can accommodate the casualty Load plus the equipment's Load. Petrified multiplies the body weight by ten, not its casualty Load. Removing gear can reduce equipment weight and Load; it cannot reduce the body's weight or erase its condition.\n\n## Meld limits across repeated transformations\n\n**New provisional meld exception:** legally melded gear is excluded from both your transformed body's active Inventory Load and its own active pound-carrying limit. Record its full Load and weight separately while it remains inaccessible. New external equipment uses the expressed form's current capacities and still needs compatible anatomy and custody.\n\nA **continuous meld chain** begins when root anatomy first enters a transformation that melds gear. At that instant, record two fixed ceilings: the pre-transformation body's ordinary active Load capacity and its ordinary physical carrying capacity in pounds. Legally melded gear must fit both. Keep the actual total Load and pounds of the hidden gear, counting containers and contents once in each quantity.\n\nDruid-to-Druid changes, internal Shapechange switches, suppression, permanent True Polymorph and continuous replacement by another spell do not end the chain or increase its ceilings. Later changes to size or Strength neither increase nor retroactively shrink these historical ceilings. An existing item is not expelled merely to make room for a new one.\n\nA later spell replacement may capture an external item only if the whole item fits the remaining room under **both** ceilings. Do not partially meld an item unless its source defines a divisible cargo quantity. A new item that will not fit remains external if compatible with the destination and within its current carrying limits; otherwise drop it or, for a forced resolution without legal placement, record its placement as pending. Excess cargo never grants immunity to a hostile or forced transformation. Druid-to-Druid changes continue to preserve their existing meld and never recapture external cargo.\n\nFor a voluntary change, declare and preflight the full item disposition before spending costs. A successful hostile or forced change resolves its mandatory custody consequences without deleting items or refunding its costs. This same overflow procedure applies at the first forced transformation if the target began with excess cargo; only gear within both starting ceilings can become hidden.\n\nWhen someone else carries the transformed character, count its casualty Load plus both its melded and external equipment Load. Count the effective physical body weight plus all that equipment for actual weight handling. Melding therefore provides no weightless cargo for another carrier.\n\nOnly an actual final return to root anatomy, with the hidden equipment released and reconciled, ends the chain. Returned gear rejoins active inventory under the root body's **current** capacities. Resolve excess with the return-and-drop procedure; overload cannot prevent a mandatory reversion. A later new transformation can then establish a new pair of ceilings from its actual legal starting state.\n\n## Extraction Class and Secure Cargo\n\nExtraction Class is a danger and logistics rating, not a carrying allowance:\n\n| Class | Cargo |\n|---|---|\n| 0 | Ordinary trade goods |\n| 1 | Common monster parts or durable salvage |\n| 2 | Uncommon, fragile or regulated cargo |\n| 3 | Rare artifacts, volatile substances or living captives |\n| 4 | Very rare strategic cargo or dangerous curses |\n| 5 | Epic cargo likely to attract sovereign or faction intervention |\n| 6 | Legendary cargo requiring a dedicated extraction operation |\n\nRetain relevant Fragile, Volatile, Cursed, Living, Contraband, Perishable and Attention tags. A light object can carry a high Extraction Class, and a heavy ordinary crate can remain Class 0.\n\n**New provisional wording for Secure Cargo:** spend an Action on one legal attach, lift, transfer or protective operation involving one discrete cargo unit or bundle with an assigned Extraction Class. The unit must be physically handleable as declared; naming an entire convoy a bundle does not make it one operation. The action grants no extra capacity, hands, equipment, automatic success, invulnerability or reduction in Extraction Class. Apply actual restraints, harnesses, checks, hazards and source-specific handling requirements.\n\n## Examples\n\n**The remote sword.** A Druid wears armor, holds a staff and carries a pack containing two potions. A spare sword is in a wagon. The armor, staff, pack and both potions meld when the Druid changes. The sword remains in the wagon. Neither potion can be consumed from the melded pack.\n\n**A compatible harness.** A transformed creature acquires a harness made for its present anatomy. A later change checks the harness against the destination form. If it does not fit, the voluntary change requires a legal drop. The harness is neither erased nor added to the old melded equipment.\n\n**The crowded hand.** A spell Humanoid acquires and holds a new weapon. When the spell ends, root anatomy can keep that weapon if legal. The original melded weapon returns to an available legal carrying position, or is dropped if necessary. It does not replace or duplicate the new weapon.\n\n**The loaded Hawk.** A Strength 9 Druid becomes Tiny with 3 Load of legally melded gear. It stays inaccessible and uses neither of the Hawk's active carrying capacities. Tiny capacity is 4.5 Load and 67.5 pounds. An additional package of 3 Load and 30 pounds in a genuinely compatible external carrying arrangement fits both capacities, but exceeds half the Load capacity of 2.25 and makes the Druid Burdened. A rescuer carrying that Hawk counts 1.5 casualty Load plus all 6 Load of equipment, totaling 7.5 Load, and separately satisfies the full body and equipment weight.\n\n**The repeated replacement.** A Strength 9 Medium character starts a chain with 8 Load and 80 pounds of legal gear. The fixed ceilings are 9 Load and 135 pounds. The remaining hidden capacity is therefore 1 Load and 55 pounds. A later spell cannot hide a new 3-Load, 30-pound package even if the current form can carry it externally. A whole 0.5-Load, 50-pound item fits both remaining ceilings; a 0.5-Load, 60-pound item fails the pound limit. Changing form or casting again does not refresh either ceiling.\n\n**The stone casualty.** A Medium ally has a documented base weight of 180 pounds and becomes Petrified. The body now weighs 1,800 pounds but still contributes 6 casualty Load. A Strength 18 Medium rescuer can normally carry 270 pounds or push, drag or lift 540 pounds, so cannot move that body alone through these ordinary procedures, even with enough spare Load capacity. Additional gear is counted separately.\n\n## Source decisions and implementation status\n\nThe [first body and equipment conference](../../../../docs/audit/2026-10-08/full-game/GPT_TEMPORAL_BODY_EQUIPMENT_CONFERENCE.md) supplies the equipment procedures. The [additional exact evidence](../../../../docs/audit/2026-10-08/full-game/CODEX_TEMPORAL_CARRYING_EVIDENCE.md) and [GPT's completed reconciliation](../../../../docs/audit/2026-10-08/full-game/GPT_TEMPORAL_CARRYING_RECONCILIATION.md) preserve the V6, Gameplay Loop and Core conflicts and their dispositions. The corrected candidate above replaces the first conference's full-cell Tiny footprint, factor-four mass proposal and automatic active Load from melded equipment. The manuscript review's chain budgets, pound exemption, borrowed-custody correction, separate inclusive quantities and neutral placement tie rule are applied here. [GPT confirmed this exact revised manuscript and its six examples](../../../../docs/audit/2026-10-08/full-game/GPT_BODY_FINAL_AND_ABJURATION_CONFERENCE.md); the [review manifest](BODY_EQUIPMENT_REVIEW_STATUS.json) records its scope.\n\nThe current Druid helper does not yet update the shared body, equipment custody or geometry. The spell helper's coarse hands check does not implement the full compatibility procedure. The finite ground-plane temporal helper does not model elevation, burrowing or Tiny subcells. The authored rules and these implementation gaps are separate; none of them enables full Time Loop or closes the whole-game review.\n"},{"file":"DescentReviewChanges/ReviewBible/docs/mechanics/EQUIPMENT_ACQUISITION_PLAYTEST.md","sha256":"406c68622102b444c6afb005e2d50cfd75171fe8d90db36a06d02df2d67bf27d","heading":"entire guide","exactText":"# Equipment and acquisition\n\n**REVIEWED authored operational PLAYTEST equipment/acquisition scope.** This guide applies the [equipment conference](../../../../docs/audit/2026-10-08/full-game/GPT_EQUIPMENT_ACQUISITION_CONFERENCE.md), including its twelve conference repairs, the [six exact-review repairs](../../../../docs/audit/2026-10-08/full-game/GPT_EQUIPMENT_EXACT_REVIEW.md), transaction rounding and poison clarification. Use the complete [source tables](EQUIPMENT_SOURCE_BINDINGS.md), [item procedures](EQUIPMENT_ITEM_PROCEDURES.md), [starting packages](EQUIPMENT_STARTING_PACKAGES.md) and [equipment data](../../data/playtest/equipment_acquisition_candidate.json) together. Source tables remain evidence where this guide explicitly replaces or completes a rule.\n\nThis covers mundane armor, weapons, tools, focuses, common gear, creation packages, object damage, repair and ordinary acquisition. Full crafting recipes, harvesting, vehicles, mounted combat and artifact/faction economy retain their separate unfinished work. Original books and alternate rules remain preserved. Cosmology is deferred until after a successful authorized GitHub push and verification of its remote commit.\n\n## Record possessions and permissions\n\nRecord each physical item's identity, quantity, actual owner/custodian, location, size, equipment grade, condition, remaining contents/charges and any explicit source exception. Ownership grants no proficiency, spell access, recipe, attunement or class feature. Training grants no free item. A price is a baseline quote, not automatic shop stock or a guaranteed buyer.\n\nThe source catalog retains unknown fields as null. Its printed price or weight can apply to a bundle, such as twenty arrows, rather than one unit. The operational projection records bundle size and unit values separately. Unarmed strike is an attack option, not an inventory object. Services and living mounts are not generic mundane objects with a weapon's durability.\n\nUse the actual equipped item and current legal class/ancestry/card permissions. A historical feature with the same name supplies no additional grant. Spells and items require their own real targets and actions. A flask cannot be thrown from inside inaccessible melded equipment.\n\n## Armor and shields\n\nChoose one applicable AC formula. Light armor adds the full DEX modifier to its printed base. Medium adds `min(DEX modifier, 2)`, including a negative modifier. Heavy uses its fixed AC without positive or negative DEX. Explicit separate AC bonuses apply only when their own requirements are met.\n\nOne usable equipped shield contributes its printed bonus: buckler +1, shield +2, tower shield +3. No second shield bonus. Elven weave's printed Stealth advantage uses ordinary advantage/disadvantage cancellation. Dwarven plate is AC 18; its descriptive superior protection adds nothing. Scale mail's description adds no slashing resistance. Tower shield adds no unprinted cover or additional movement penalty.\n\n**Nonproficient worn armor.** Retain V6 disadvantage on STR/DEX ability checks, saving throws and attacks. Core replaces the older casting prohibition: spells remain permitted, but an RP-priced spell costs +1 RP once per cast and concentration checks/saves have disadvantage. Multiple nonproficient armor pieces never multiply this surcharge. A normal 0-RP spell using the RP price interface can cost 1 RP. Apply this ordinary price addition before the already reviewed eligible reductions and final Resonance Influence step.\n\n**Nonproficient shield — new completion.** While using a shield without shield proficiency, apply the STR/DEX check/save/attack disadvantage. A shield alone adds neither Core's armor RP surcharge nor concentration disadvantage and does not prohibit casting. Core names worn armor for those casting penalties.\n\nA charge-only ancestry spell, Oracle effect or other cast with no RP-price interface acquires no RP price or pool from nonproficient armor. Actual concentration still has disadvantage from nonproficient worn armor. Hands, spell components and other independent casting restrictions remain.\n\n**Strength minimum — new completion.** If one or more equipped armor/shield records have a listed STR minimum you do not meet, reduce Speed by 10 feet total, minimum 0. This is not a minimum to equip; multiple deficient pieces do not stack the reduction. This penalty can coexist with Load/Burden.\n\nThe Dwarf's printed rule that Speed is not reduced by wearing heavy armor prevents this penalty when the deficient item is heavy armor. It does not waive Load/Burden, another movement penalty or a tower shield's separate STR minimum. If a table chooses V6's preserved optional encumbrance alternate, that alternate ignores the Armor Table Strength column; the active reviewed Load procedure is not that alternate.\n\n| Equipment | Don | Doff |\n|---|---|---|\n| Light armor | 1 minute | 1 minute |\n| Medium armor | 5 minutes | 1 minute |\n| Heavy armor | 10 minutes | 5 minutes |\n| Any shield | 1 Action | 1 Action |\n\nOne actual assisting creature halves doff time only; a shield still takes at least one Action. Additional helpers do not repeatedly halve it.\n\n## Hands and focuses\n\nA buckler straps to one forearm. Its partially available hand can carry a small nonweapon object, but supplies no free S hand, loading hand, second-weapon attack, two-handed weapon support or active focus manipulation. An ordinary shield or tower shield occupies one hand. Soldier Shield Trained is a specific selected-feature exception for its repeating-crossbow/strapped-shield use, with that feature's actual reload requirements.\n\nA two-handed weapon requires both hands when attacking with it. Releasing one grip costs no Action; regripping is part of its next legal use. This does not free a hand already occupied by another object, restraint or incompatible anatomy.\n\nF requires an actual focus legal for the casting source. A hand holding only that focus may manipulate it and supply S for the same cast; the focus itself is not a conflicting object. A weapon, shield, second object, loading requirement or other hand restriction can make that hand unavailable. An item independently serving as both weapon and focus can use this rule only when an actual source authorizes that item as the caster's focus.\n\nA holy symbol may supply F while held, visibly worn or borne on a shield where that casting source permits holy symbols. A worn or shield-borne symbol does not supply a free S hand. V still requires actual audible speech, and neither a focus nor component pouch waives explicitly priced or consumed materials. The component pouch supplies its ordinary storage function; this guide does not give it a new universal F substitution.\n\nThe following focus assignments are **new class integration** for actual legal class spells requiring F. They grant no spells or resource pools.
